and 0.03448 (0.03427--0.03468). The retrospectively best budget equaled
the imposed rank on every confirmation task, while the contiguous balanced
tree won 308/320 feedback-only and 303/320 joint tasks. This supports a
restricted rank--capacity relation in the constructed task class; it supplies
little evidence for fine tree selection.

We then examined why the selector failed, without changing the primary
analysis or its records. Exact expected
calibration one-step decrease, using the full Hessian and actual minibatch
covariance, satisfied the local bound in all 12,800 conditions (minimum
slack $3.48\times10^{-5}$). Reconstructing each first training update gave
mean within-task rank correlations of initialization utility with independent-
test decrease of 0.98369 (0.98069--0.98638) and 0.97391
(0.96951--0.97755). Thus local prediction can succeed while the calibrated
long-horizon selector fails. Laplacian and Schur identities, zero-coupling
equivalence, exact spectrum invariance, minibatch covariance and finite-
difference checks passed; 120 independently replayed endpoints agreed to
$5.55\times10^{-17}$. Frozen configurations, full matrices, choices,
outcomes and audits are in
\texttt{source\_data/prospective\_morphology\_selection/}.

\input{morphology_followup_methods}

\input{credit_first_bridge_methods}

\subsection{State-matched positive-conductance reliability experiment}

We next tested whether positive conductances could realize the attenuation
prescribed by Eq.~\ref{eq:s_reliability_gain} in a separate trained branch
model (Table~\ref{tab:positive_reliability}). Here $b$ indexes branches, $i$ examples and $d$ inputs within a branch. Every
input rate $x_{bid}$ was nonnegative, and each excitatory conductance was
parameterized by $g_{bd}=\operatorname{softplus}(q_{bd})$, with trainable
raw parameter $q_{bd}$. With leak conductance $g^L$, define total unshunted
conductance and voltage on example $i$
\begin{equation}
G_{bi}=g^L+\sum_d x_{bid}g_{bd},\qquad
V_{bi}=\frac{\sum_d x_{bid}g_{bd}}{G_{bi}}.
\label{eq:s_positive_branch_voltage}
\end{equation}
A fixed shunt $\kappa_b\geq0$ had reversal zero. To isolate its effect on
input resistance from its effect on the forward feature, an oracle current
$\kappa_bV_{bi}$ was added on each example. The perturbed state is therefore
\begin{equation}
V_{bi}'=\frac{\sum_d x_{bid}g_{bd}+\kappa_bV_{bi}}{G_{bi}+\kappa_b}=V_{bi},
\qquad
\frac{\partial V_{bi}'}{\partial q_{bd}}
=\frac{x_{bid}(1-V_{bi})}{G_{bi}+\kappa_b}\,
\operatorname{sigmoid}(q_{bd}).
\label{eq:s_state_matched_shunt}
\end{equation}
Relative to the unshunted eligibility, the example-specific physical
input-resistance factor is $G_{bi}/(G_{bi}+\kappa_b)$. The compensating current was recomputed from the
pre-shunt state and treated as a clamp, not differentiated as an independently
learned pathway. Consequently this manipulation deliberately removes forward
feature suppression while retaining conductance attenuation in the local
update.

The binary task used eight branches, four inputs per branch, 512 training
examples and 2,048 held-out examples. Input rates were generated from a
positive baseline plus a class-dependent branch pattern and clipped at zero.
Initial full-data gradients supplied
$S_b=\lVert\bm g_b\rVert_2^2$. Coefficient-noise variance was then scaled so
that
\begin{equation}
N_b=S_b\exp(-2h x_b),\qquad x_b\in[-1,1],
\end{equation}
where $x_b$ is the evenly spaced branch-order coordinate and
$h\in\{0,0.5,1,1.5,2\}$ controls reliability heterogeneity. The statistical reliability was
$r_b=S_b/(S_b+N_b)$, lower-bounded at 0.03 for numerical stability. Because
the common step was $\eta=c/L$ with $c=0.5$, the corrected fixed-step target
was $a_b^*=\min\{1,r_b/c\}$ and the initial shunt was
$\kappa_b=\overline G_b[(a_b^*)^{-1}-1]$, where $\overline G_b$ is the
training-example mean of $G_{bi}$ at initialization. Shunts remained fixed during the 40
updates; the excitatory conductance parameters were trained, and the same
coefficient-noise realizations were used across matched methods within seed.

Nine conditions separated the relevant alternatives: exact clean
backpropagation, noisy credit without a shunt, a state-matched
unattenuated-eligibility control, the step-consistent best single global shunt,
reliability-aligned, seed-shuffled and anti-aligned shunts, an explicit
point-gain control receiving the identical branch factors, and aligned
shunting with clean credit. The point-gain control is a resource
control, not a biological assertion: because it receives the same branch
partition and factors, its update must equal Eq.~\ref{eq:s_state_matched_shunt}.
The clean-backpropagation condition retains the unattenuated exact gradient and
is the optimization reference.

An exploratory implementation used $r_b$ despite taking $c=0.5$ and was
excluded from inference. After correcting the target factor, we fixed the
analysis plan, evaluated two implementation-check seeds (also excluded), and
then used 50 paired analysis seeds (8100--8149). All 2,250 expected
seed--condition rows completed. The minimum input rate was zero, maximum state
mismatch was $2.22\times10^{-16}$, and independently computed point-gain and
unattenuated-eligibility trajectory differences were $4.00\times10^{-15}$ and
$1.31\times10^{-14}$. A finite-difference validation
held the compensating current fixed while perturbing an excitatory parameter;
its maximum relative error was $3.60\times10^{-8}$. All prespecified numerical
criteria were met.

At $h=2$, reliability-aligned minus best-global one-step test-loss reduction
was 0.001817 (95\% paired seed-bootstrap interval 0.001618--0.002020), positive
in 49/50 seeds (two-sided Wilcoxon $P=3.55\times10^{-15}$). Aligned minus
unshunted reduction averaged 0.001339 (0.000594--0.002096), positive in
33/50 seeds ($P=0.0030$). After 40 updates, aligned-minus-global test loss was
$-0.020404$ ($-0.022220$ to $-0.018705$), favoring aligned in 50/50 seeds
($P=1.78\times10^{-15}$). Aligned shunting also had lower final loss than
shuffled and anti-aligned shunts by 0.009204 and 0.013781, respectively.
However, aligned-minus-unshunted final loss was 0.000425
($-0.002456$--0.003071; $P=0.154$), so the immediate improvement did not
establish a sustained loss difference from no shunt. Exact clean
backpropagation retained the lowest mean final loss. These results validate a
positive-conductance approximation and ordering of fixed-step oracle reliability factors
under a state clamp; they do not establish autonomous shunt learning,
dendrite-exclusive computation or general superiority to unshunted learning.

\subsection{Adaptive local reliability estimation}

The preceding experiment supplied the reliability moments. We next asked
whether they could be estimated from noisy local observations, specifying
the adaptive-gain experiment before examining its outcomes
(Table~\ref{tab:adaptive_reliability}). At the current parameter
state, branch $b$ received two conditionally independent noisy local gradient
observations, $\widehat{\bm g}_{1b}$ and $\widehat{\bm g}_{2b}$, on the same
examples. It formed
\begin{equation}
 \widehat S_b^{\mathrm{obs}}
 =\langle\widehat{\bm g}_{1b},\widehat{\bm g}_{2b}\rangle,
 \qquad
 \widehat N_b^{\mathrm{obs}}
 =\frac12\lVert\widehat{\bm g}_{1b}-\widehat{\bm g}_{2b}\rVert_2^2.
\label{eq:s_adaptive_reliability_moments}
\end{equation}
For independent zero-mean credit noise, these estimate the squared clean
branch gradient and the noise energy of one observation. Eight paired
observations initialized the moments; exponential averages with decay 0.9
were then updated before each of 40 training steps. Negative finite-sample
signal estimates and moments below $10^{-12}$ were clipped only when the gain
was constructed. The step-consistent gain followed
Eq.~\ref{eq:s_reliability_gain}, was bounded to $[0.03,1]$, and was converted
to a shunt using the branch's current mean total conductance.

The task and state-matched positive-conductance model were unchanged. Three
heterogeneity levels $h\in\{0,1,2\}$ crossed exact clean backpropagation,
noisy no-shunt learning, the fixed initial-oracle shunt, adaptive local,
adaptive global and fixed-permutation adaptive shunts, and an independently
computed adaptive point gain. Main update noise and estimator-probe noise were
paired across methods. Two implementation-check seeds were excluded from
inference; the analysis then used 50 paired seeds (8200--8249), producing all 1,050 planned
seed--condition outcomes. Maximum state error was
$2.22\times10^{-16}$, maximum physical-shunt finite-difference error was
$4.01\times10^{-8}$, and the adaptive physical-shunt/point-gain trajectories
agreed within $4.00\times10^{-15}$.

At $h=2$, adaptive-local minus adaptive-global one-step loss decrease was
0.001420 (95\% paired-seed bootstrap interval 0.001158--0.001684; 45/50
positive; two-sided Wilcoxon $P=4.49\times10^{-13}$). Adaptive local reduced
final loss relative to adaptive global by 0.016217
(0.014501--0.017913; 50/50 seeds; $P=1.78\times10^{-15}$) and relative to
shuffled adaptive placement by 0.005528 (0.003766--0.007418; 41/50;
$P=7.80\times10^{-8}$). The one-step local-minus-shuffled mean was positive,
but only 26/50 signs favored local and therefore did not meet the predefined
directional criterion.

Adaptive estimation nevertheless performed worse than the no-shunt and
fixed-oracle references. Adaptive-local final loss exceeded no
shunt by 0.004250 (0.002499--0.005975; 39/50; $P=1.78\times10^{-5}$) and the
fixed initial oracle by 0.003449 (0.002577--0.004305; 43/50;
$P=4.44\times10^{-9}$). The adaptive final gain ordering had mean within-seed
Spearman $\rho=0.756$ with the initial oracle and mean absolute gain error
0.205. Thus local estimates identified useful relative gain placements, but did
not provide a sustained advantage over unshunted learning. Paired gradient probes,
known optimizer step fraction and the exact state clamp remain supplied; this
is not a kinetic or fully autonomous model of inhibitory plasticity.

\subsection{Same-span noisy coefficient-learning experiment}

To isolate coefficient learning from address capacity, we compared three
dictionaries with the same span in a rate-based factorial
(Table~\ref{tab:same_span_learning}). The 16-leaf balanced-tree Haar basis supplied the common
rank-eight span through depth three. The tested dictionaries were the eight
orthonormal Haar modes, all 15 nested indicators through depth three, and the
same nested indicators scaled by positive gains evenly spaced on the log scale
from $\exp(-3)$ to $\exp(3)$. The dictionaries' projectors agreed to
$9.44\times10^{-16}$ and their positive-spectrum Gram condition numbers were
1, 15 and 388.52.

Each independent seed drew a unit-norm target in the common span. At every
update the estimator observed the target plus isotropic Gaussian noise with
standard deviation $2/\sqrt{n_{\mathrm{eff}}}$, where
$n_{\mathrm{eff}}\in\{4,16,64,256\}$. The same 80 observation vectors were
used for every matched parameterization. Vanilla coefficient descent used
$0.2/\lambda_{\max}(\Phi^{\mathsf T}\Phi)$; the oracle coordinate control used
$0.2(\Phi^{\mathsf T}\Phi)^+$. Population field loss was recorded after 1, 5, 20
and 80 updates. The target address residual was below
$1.45\times10^{-30}$ in every condition, so all reported loss is within-span
coefficient-estimation error.

The predictions were specified before two implementation-check seeds, which
were excluded from inference, and 50 paired seeds (9100--9149). The results contradicted the prediction that Haar coordinates would
always reduce finite-time error. At $n_{\mathrm{eff}}=4$, raw-minus-Haar
final loss was $-0.2774$ (95\% paired interval $-0.3260$ to $-0.2313$) and
scaled-minus-Haar was $-0.1906$ ($-0.2500$ to $-0.1335$): slower modes
filtered high estimator noise. At $n_{\mathrm{eff}}=256$, the effects reversed
to 0.02555 (0.02171--0.02952) and 0.17091 (0.14576--0.19686), positive in
50/50 seeds for both contrasts. Equation~\ref{eq:s_coefficient_finite_risk},
derived after these outcomes to explain the reversal, predicted median
crossovers at effective
sample sizes 38.53 and 8.25. The Gram-preconditioned field trajectories agreed
across dictionaries to $1.15\times10^{-15}$. Thus static parameterization can
alter finite-data learning without altering the attainable credit field.
\section{Artificial-tree experiments}
\label{note:artificial}

The exact conductance gradient separates local eligibility from transported
error. We used controlled artificial trees to ask how much of that error must
be resolved: neuron identity, within-arbor path identity, or both. We began
with regular directed morphologies and increased feedback resolution while
holding the forward task fixed within each comparison.

\subsection{Architecture and input sparsity}

MNIST pixels were divided by 255 and flattened to 784 inputs, without
further normalization or augmentation. The 60,000-image training set was split by seed into
48,000 training and 12,000 validation images; the official 10,000-image test
set was evaluated only after training. The core contained 128 modeled
excitatory neurons and no separate inhibitory-neuron population. Each neuron
had morphology $[3,3]$: three proximal compartments, three distal children per
proximal compartment and one soma, for 13 compartments per neuron. There were
no somatic input synapses.

At each of the 12 dendritic compartments, separate sparse parameter arrays (TopK)
selected 40 excitatory and 20 inhibitory contacts from the same 784-pixel
input. The model therefore contained 92,160 active input contacts among
2,408,448 candidate synaptic parameters. Inactive local weights were not
updated. Child-to-parent couplings and all active conductances were learned
after a softplus transform. A linear $128\times10$ decoder produced the class
scores. The archived model-resource record contained 2,414,602 trainable raw
parameters in total, including candidate synaptic arrays, couplings,
reactivation parameters and decoder.

\subsection{Raw-additive and normalized-additive controls}

The raw-additive control used the configuration alias
\texttt{dendritic\_additive}, with shunting and additive normalization both
disabled. For compartment $n$, the implemented forward computation was
\begin{align}
J_n^{\mathrm E,\mathrm{add}}
&=\sum_{i\in\mathcal S_n^{\mathrm E}}g_i^{\mathrm E}x_i^{\mathrm E},
&J_n^{\mathrm I,\mathrm{add}}
&=\sum_{j\in\mathcal S_n^{\mathrm I}}g_j^{\mathrm I}x_j^{\mathrm I},\\
J_n^{\mathrm{child},\mathrm{add}}
&=\sum_{c\in\mathcal C_n}g_{c\to n}^{\mathrm{den}}a_c,
&V_n^{\mathrm{add}}
&=J_n^{\mathrm E,\mathrm{add}}-J_n^{\mathrm I,\mathrm{add}}
+J_n^{\mathrm{child},\mathrm{add}},
\qquad a_n=f_n(V_n^{\mathrm{add}}).
\label{eq:s_additive_forward}
\end{align}
Consequently,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\partial V_n^{\mathrm{add}}}{\partial g_i^{\mathrm E}}=x_i^{\mathrm E},
\quad
\frac{\partial V_n^{\mathrm{add}}}{\partial g_j^{\mathrm I}}=-x_j^{\mathrm I},
\quad
\frac{\partial V_n^{\mathrm{add}}}{\partial g_{c\to n}^{\mathrm{den}}}=a_c,
\quad
\delta_c^V=\delta_n^Vg_{c\to n}^{\mathrm{den}}f_c'(V_c).
\label{eq:s_additive_derivatives}
\end{equation}
This control retained excitatory currents and subtractive inhibitory currents
on the same branching graph, with the same reactivation function. It omitted
the conductance denominator, reversal-potential driving force and
parent-compartment resistance. Its
inhibitory term is a negative current contribution, not a negative
conductance. Raw-parameter gradients in
both architectures also included the softplus derivative.

The main regular-tree experiments use this raw-additive model. Supplementary
Figs.~S1--S3 and S4A,B instead use a stage-normalized variant that standardizes the raw
voltage separately within each example \(s\) across the output coordinates
\(n\) of every stage. Writing
\(Z_{sn}=J_{sn}^{\mathrm E,\mathrm{add}}
-J_{sn}^{\mathrm I,\mathrm{add}}
+J_{sn}^{\mathrm{child},\mathrm{add}}\),
the normalized model is
\begin{equation}
V_{sn}^{\mathrm{norm}}=
\frac{Z_{sn}-\langle Z_{s\cdot}\rangle_n}
{\operatorname{sd}_n(Z_{s\cdot})+\epsilon},
\qquad
a_{sn}=\frac{\tanh(0.1V_{sn}^{\mathrm{norm}})+1}{2}.
\label{eq:s_historical_normalized_additive}
\end{equation}
Its reactivation slope and midpoint were fixed at \(m=0.1\) and \(b=0\), respectively. We call this
the \emph{normalized-additive model}. Because normalization changes the
forward computation, its absolute accuracies are not treated as repeated
measurements of the raw-additive model.

We verified the normalized-additive implementation by reproducing its
backpropagation endpoints. Across five seeds, normalized-additive and shunting
networks reached 48.300\% and 49.542\% on flattened CIFAR-10, compared with
reference values of 48.300\% and 49.516\%. We then selected a raw-additive backpropagation (BP)
configuration using validation accuracy only. Adaptive initialization,
empirical reactivation calibration and the same optimizer settings reached
49.960\% validation accuracy and 49.723\% test accuracy across three
calibration seeds. This procedure defines the operating point for the
independent learning-rule comparison; it does not separate initialization from
reactivation calibration and is not itself a comparison among learning rules.
A provisional raw-additive configuration with a matched-BP mean of 38.98\%
was excluded because it did not provide an adequate operating point.

In the MNIST, Fashion-MNIST and depth experiments, every compartment used a
learned reactivation function with slope $m_n$ and midpoint $b_n$:
\begin{equation}
f_n(V)=\frac{\tanh[m_n(V-b_n)]+1}{2},\qquad
f_n'(V)=\frac{m_n}{2}\left\{1-\tanh^2[m_n(V-b_n)]\right\}.
\label{eq:s_parametric_tanh}
\end{equation}
The shunting architecture used its analytical initialization policy. Because
the raw-additive voltage scale differs, the raw-additive architecture used its
predefined occupancy-quantile policy: the empirical 0.1 and 0.9 voltage
quantiles were mapped to reactivation outputs 0.1 and 0.9, with minimum
quantile width $10^{-3}$, maximum slope 50 and reversion on an invalid fit.
These were architecture-specific, prespecified reactivation-initialization
policies rather than identical initial values. The independent raw-additive CIFAR-10
confirmation instead used the coupled empirical-calibration procedure selected
by the validation-only screen above. All runs met the predefined completeness,
calibration, convergence and backpropagation-adequacy criteria. The
prespecified neuron-specific bandwidth criterion was met, whereas the
prespecified exact-path superiority criterion was not
(Supplementary Fig.~S4D).

\subsection{Training protocols and feedback cohorts}

The primary feedback-definition experiment used the three-factor rule, a
locally updated decoder, mini-batches of 256 and 180 epochs. Adam used
$\beta_1=0.9$, $\beta_2=0.999$, $\epsilon=10^{-8}$ and no weight decay. Raw
TopK parameters, child couplings, reactivation parameters and decoder used
learning rates $1.5\times10^{-3}$, $7\times10^{-4}$,
$5\times10^{-4}$ and $1.5\times10^{-3}$, respectively. Local gradients were
clipped elementwise at 5. The best validation checkpoint was restored; early
stopping and mixed precision were disabled. Runs used deterministic PyTorch
settings, disabled TensorFloat-32 (TF32) and cuDNN benchmarking, and used the highest float32
matrix-multiplication precision.

The original feedback ladder retained in Supplementary Fig.~S45 used 15 seeds (42--56) for each of three feedback
fields---strict scalar feedback, neuron-specific feedback and exact path
transport---in each architecture (90 runs). A matched-width scalar-fallback
condition at the same seeds and architectures added 30 runs. The decision to
replace an earlier mixed-source set with these 120 consistently executed runs
was made before their outcomes were examined. Versioned configurations,
checkpoints and source identifiers are retained for all runs; the superseded
results are not used in the article.

The Fashion-MNIST replication retained these shunting and raw-additive
architectures, contact pattern,
three-factor local rule, local decoder, optimizer, 180-epoch limit and
architecture-specific initialization without retuning. Images were flattened
to 784 inputs without normalization or augmentation. For each paired seed
10200--10209, 48,000 of the 60,000 training images were used for fitting and
12,000 for validation; the official 10,000-image test set was used only after
checkpoint selection. Sixty fits crossed shunting and raw-additive trees with
matched-width scalar-fallback feedback, neuron-specific feedback and exact path
transport.

Mean accuracies for matched-width scalar-fallback, neuron-specific and exact
path fields were 0.8393,
0.8839 and 0.8829 in shunting trees and 0.8442, 0.8820 and 0.8833 in
raw-additive trees. Neuron-specific feedback improved over matched-width
scalar-fallback feedback by
4.46 percentage points (95\% paired-seed interval 4.09--4.88) in shunting trees and 3.78 points
(3.43--4.14) in raw-additive trees; every seed difference was positive in both
architectures (exact two-sided sign-flip $P=0.001953$). Exact path minus
neuron-specific feedback was $-0.11$ points ($-0.21$ to $-0.01$; 2/10 positive;
$P=0.0957$) and 0.13 points ($-0.08$ to 0.35; 8/10 positive; $P=0.314$),
respectively. The benefit of preserving neuron identity therefore replicated, whereas
within-tree transport added no reliable benefit on this dataset
(Supplementary Fig.~S4E).

The prespecified replication criterion required at least 8/10 positive
neuron-specific-minus-scalar-fallback differences and an interval above zero in each architecture. All 60 runs
produced finite endpoints and complete checkpoints at the specified seeds,
without activating a fallback pathway. Seed-level outcomes, contrasts and
versioned source identifiers are provided in
\texttt{source\_data/fashion\_feedback\_ladder/}.

\input{image_ladder_controls_methods}

\subsection{Raw-additive CIFAR-10 feedback ladder}

The CIFAR-10 analysis used flattened inputs without normalization or
augmentation and a 90:10 training--validation split. The model contained one
20-soma dendritic population with branch factors \([3,3,3,3]\), 25 direct
contacts of each input sign per branch and a 20--32--16--10 decoder. With
\texttt{input\_mode=1}, the stored inhibitory-population fields are inactive
unless a separate build flag is enabled. That flag was absent, so the realized
network had direct excitatory and inhibitory input streams but no separate
inhibitory-cell population. This architecture was verified from the source
before the accuracies were examined.

A validation-only backpropagation screen selected adaptive network
initialization together with empirical per-layer reactivation calibration from
three training batches. Because these choices were selected jointly, the
experiment does not attribute recovery to either component alone. All four
feedback conditions used the resulting raw-additive forward model, Adam at
\(10^{-3}\), batch size 256, at most 200 epochs, early-stopping patience 40 and
restoration of the validation-best checkpoint. Adam's built-in weight decay was
zero. The separate configuration field equal to 0.01 controlled sparse
active-weight maintenance rather than L2 regularization. Twenty paired analysis
seeds (10800--10819) crossed strict scalar, neuron-specific, exact-path and
matched-backpropagation feedback, for 80 fits.

All 80 runs produced finite endpoints, complete checkpoints and empirical
calibration records while preserving the specified seed pairing; no convergence
flags occurred. Mean test accuracies were 0.34472
for strict scalar feedback, 0.50865 for neuron-specific feedback, 0.50006 for exact path
transport and 0.50122 for matched backpropagation. Neuron-specific sharing minus strict scalar feedback
was 0.16393 (95\% paired-\(t\) interval, 0.15878--0.16908; 20/20 positive;
Holm-adjusted directional \(P=5.57\times10^{-24}\); exact sign-flip
\(P=9.54\times10^{-7}\)). Exact path minus neuron-specific was \(-0.00859\)
(\(-0.01171\) to \(-0.00547\); 2/20 positive), so every prespecified
exact-path superiority criterion failed. Exact path minus backpropagation was
\(-0.00115\) (95\% interval, \(-0.00457\) to 0.00226) and passed a formal equivalence assessment using two one-sided tests (TOST)
within \(\pm0.01\) (90\% interval, \(-0.00398\) to 0.00167;
\(P_{\mathrm{TOST}}=1.58\times10^{-5}\)). Thus this harder-data cohort
confirms a feedback-bandwidth benefit at the neuron-identity level but not an
advantage of exact within-arbor transport. It is a compact transfer assay, not
a competitive CIFAR-10 benchmark.

The exact-transport factorial was a separate five-seed protocol (seeds
42--46): it used 200 epochs, a rectified linear unit (ReLU) core output, and crossed three- versus
five-factor eligibility with local versus backpropagated decoder updates.
TopK, coupling, reactivation and decoder learning rates were
$1.5\times10^{-3}$, $7\times10^{-4}$, $5\times10^{-4}$ and
$1.5\times10^{-3}$. Its same-architecture backpropagation reference used the
same seeds, 200-epoch budget, ReLU output, and validation-based checkpoint
selection, with method-specific learning rates of $10^{-3}$ for TopK and
decoder and $5\times10^{-4}$ for coupling and reactivation. Test data were not used for
checkpoint selection. Tables~\ref{tab:regular_architecture}, \ref{tab:regular_training} and
\ref{tab:regular_archive} provide the complete specifications and provenance;
Table~\ref{tab:regular_results} reports the principal outcomes.

Across the two tested architectures, preserving neuron-specific feedback
recovered most of the scalar-feedback deficit. This comparison does not imply
that shunting universally improves learning. The separate exact-transport
experiment establishes that local three-factor eligibility is sufficient when
the remaining compartment error is supplied accurately.

We compared the analytic gradient with automatic differentiation in 100
diagnostic runs. Median relative error was
$7.43\times10^{-8}$ and the maximum was $1.88\times10^{-7}$. Parameter
tensors, rather than scalar tensor elements, were the diagnostic units.

\subsection{Depth-by-feedback experiment and fixed-checkpoint diagnostic}

Table~\ref{tab:input_validity} accounts for all planned, excluded and
included units in this and the related artificial-tree cohorts.
This experiment crossed two tasks, raw-additive and shunting neuron
models, one to four dendritic stages, and three local teaching fields at ten
paired seeds. A
matched backpropagation condition was included for each
task--model--depth--seed combination, giving 480 local-learning and 160
backpropagation runs. The local conditions all used the theorem-derived
three-factor eligibility. One modeled population of 128 neurons was used so
that equal neuron indices in different feedforward populations could not share
a teaching coordinate. The design, configurations, seeds, inclusion criteria
and analysis code were specified before the outcomes were examined.

Runs were included if they contained the specified configuration and seed, a
checkpoint, complete learning curves, all expected dendritic stages,
nondegenerate learned couplings and finite held-out metrics, without a
transport-shape fallback. All 640 runs met these completion criteria. A later,
outcome-independent conductance-validity criterion excluded the 160 synthetic-noise
shunting runs because their
signed transfer output is not a valid positive-conductance drive. The 480
retained runs contain both neuron models on MNIST and the raw-additive model on
the noise task. Nominal depth changed compartment and parameter count and is
therefore interpreted as a depth stress rather than a capacity-matched
comparison. Table~\ref{tab:prospective_learning} summarizes the retained
feedback contrasts.

To separate feedback dimension from assignment, an additional prespecified cohort
compared correct neuron-specific feedback with a fixed derangement at depths two
and four. The derangement preserves the number and per-example distribution of
teaching coordinates, the forward network, and the training path; only the
coordinate-to-tree address changes. Applying the same validity rule removed
the 40 synthetic-noise shunting runs. Table~\ref{tab:matched_routing} reports
the six retained paired contrasts from 120 runs.

A second prespecified cohort tested inhibitory dose on the noise-resilience task. It
crossed two neuron models, inhibitory-contact counts of 0, 5, 10, 20 and 40 per
branch, backpropagation or three local feedback fields, and ten paired seeds,
for 400 runs. Its primary endpoint was the change from dose 0 to dose 40 in the
shunting-minus-raw-additive accuracy difference. Because all 200 shunting cells
used the invalid signed-input convention, the full family is excluded from
figures and inference. Its configurations and exclusion status are retained in
Source Data.

\subsection{Exact transport, spatial connectivity and depth controls}

We repeated the exact-path-transport versus backpropagation comparison with
valid conductance inputs. The factorial design crossed the two learning
methods with MNIST or the three-class noisy-line task (defined below), raw-additive or
positive-conductance shunting cores, depths one through four and seeds 42--51.
The analysis therefore contained 320 training runs and 160 method pairs. All
included runs used source commit
\texttt{74792ca9\allowbreak 17240661\allowbreak 48f36c75\allowbreak 8191744a\allowbreak 315d62b3}; the nested synthetic-data
repository used commit
\texttt{4f3612a5\allowbreak 2756c369\allowbreak 1e5bd269\allowbreak c6465c02\allowbreak f240c1e8}. The raw-additive synthetic model
retained its signed transfer output. The positive-conductance shunting model
used an explicit ReLU output transfer for both learning methods before
conductances entered the denominator. This rerun therefore uses
valid nonnegative shunting inputs and is not a direct re-execution of the
excluded signed-input conditions.

Runs were included if they contained the specified configuration and seed, a
complete learning summary, finite final metrics and a finite stage-complete
checkpoint, without a nonfinite training event or transport-shape fallback. If device memory was insufficient only to compute a metric on the full
dataset at once, the
same final metric was recomputed in streaming batches and still had to be
finite. Initial attempts that lacked the required nested data repository, had
insufficient memory or reached the signed-shunting input check produced no
endpoint and were excluded independently of outcome. Missing configurations
were re-executed without changing their scientific settings. Inference paired exact path transport and
backpropagation by seed within task, core and depth. The four plotted task--core
effects first averaged the four depths within seed and then used a 20,000-draw
paired-seed bootstrap interval; two-sided Wilcoxon tests are supplied as
descriptive method-agreement checks rather than equivalence tests.
All 320 runs met the inclusion criteria. Forty-two used streaming recomputation
for at least one terminal metric; every included final
metric was finite. Table~\ref{tab:clean_exact_bp} reports the four
depth-averaged comparisons. Across all 160 depth-specific method pairs, mean
exact-minus-backpropagation accuracy was $-0.0545$ percentage points and the
largest absolute individual pair difference was $5.90$ points. The latter
precludes interpreting the analysis as pointwise equality even though each
depth-averaged task--core interval included zero.

To test the role of input organization, a separate cohort compared fixed
spatial and random sparse input maps at depth four. The 320 runs crossed two tasks, two neuron models, backpropagation or
three local feedback fields, two maps, and ten paired seeds. Each distal branch
had 21 contacts. Spatial branches recursively partitioned the $28\times28$
feature plane into 16 disjoint regions per neuron, whereas random branches
sampled independently from all 784 coordinates. Supplementary
Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_spatial_audit} reports the 240 conductance-valid learning
contrasts together with the corresponding coverage analysis. MNIST retains
both cores; the noise task retains the raw-additive core. Because the effect
persists under backpropagation and on the randomly projected task, it is
treated as a forward sparse-connectivity prior rather than credit-routing
evidence.

A separate prespecified cohort isolated depth from terminal-branch count by
holding 16 terminal branches and an approximately fixed input contact budget. The four depth conditions contained
960, 960, 968 and 960 active contacts per soma, respectively. It crossed two
neuron models, backpropagation or three local feedback fields, four depths and
ten paired seeds, giving 320 runs. The conductance-valid synthetic comparison retains
the 160 raw-additive runs. This control matches terminal branches and contacts,
not trainable parameters: deeper networks still contain more compartments and
more coupling and reactivation parameters. Supplementary
Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_fixed_budget} reports their complete depth profiles and the
prespecified depth-four-minus-depth-one contrasts.

We then evaluated matched-width scalar-fallback, neuron-specific and exact
teaching fields on one fixed held-out batch, at each of the 120
conductance-valid backpropagation checkpoints. Only nonsomatic stages
and their trainable branch parameters entered the field and gradient vectors.
For each candidate field we computed scaled error-field capture,
eligibility-weighted gradient capture and cosine, and the loss change after
matching its update norm to the exact gradient. The primary relative step was
$10^{-5}$; $10^{-6}$, $10^{-4}$ and $10^{-3}$ are retained as sensitivity
values. The association interval resampled checkpoints while keeping paired
feedback fields together. The diagnostic did not update or select checkpoints.
Table~\ref{tab:feedback_step} reports the primary operating point.
Supplementary Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_mechanism} displays the complete diagnostic.

\subsection{Context-gated Fashion-MNIST protocol}

The context-gated task used Fashion-MNIST classes 0 and 6. Inputs were
average-pooled to $7\times7$, standardized using training data and augmented
with a constant coordinate. Each seed used
8,192 training and 2,000 test draws with replacement from the official binary
splits, balanced across context and label. Branch counts were $B=2,4,8$;
conflict doses were $0,0.25,0.5,4/7,0.6,2/3,0.75,1$. Thresholding one
underlying uniform mask made conflict nested across doses. Correct, shared,
cyclically deranged, BP and gated-point rules reused inputs and initialization.
Full-batch descent used 250 updates at step 0.04 and an initial weight
standard deviation (SD) of 0.01.
Three exploratory seeds (100--102) selected a stable setting; an additional
implementation-check seed was also excluded. Analysis seeds 11000--11019
were fixed before outcomes, with no outcome-dependent stopping.

The primary estimand was each seed's least-squares slope of
correct-minus-shared accuracy on conflict, separately for each $B$.
Prespecified criteria required positive slopes in at least 16/20 seeds,
at least ten percentage points of separation at full conflict, less than
two points at zero conflict, correct routing above derangement and exact
BP/gated-point agreement. All criteria passed. The maximum analytic-versus-
autodifferentiation discrepancy was $3.43\times10^{-7}$; equivalent-rule
accuracy differences were zero. Intervals used 20,000 paired-seed bootstrap
draws; tests were two-sided Wilcoxon tests. Mean-field assumptions and empirical
chance crossings are distinguished below.


The original 20 branch-conflict seeds were reexecuted to measure gradient
geometry throughout learning, using the same task configuration and inputs.
The original shared filesystem cache was unavailable before data loading;
the same official Fashion-MNIST files were copied to a readable cache and
verified against their published MD5 values. Only the cache location changed.
The input SHA manifest is retained in
\texttt{analysis/\allowbreak completion\_20260905/\allowbreak fashion\_cache\_manifest.json}.
Held-out diagnostic batches were evaluated at epochs 0, 1, 10, 50, 100 and
250. Candidate fields were compared both at each method's own state and at
the common exact-rule state, yielding 28,800 diagnostic rows. Maximum drift
from archived endpoints was $4.99\times10^{-11}$ in accuracy and
$4.98\times10^{-9}$ in loss. Supplementary
Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_path_demand}D--F displays common-state gradient cosine
at epochs 0, 50 and 250; all states and checkpoints remain in
\texttt{source\_data/review\_branch\_trajectories/}.
At conflict dose 0.5, shared-gradient cosine fell from 0.987 to $-0.519$
for four branches and from 0.952 to $-0.701$ for eight branches by epoch
250. These changes show why the centered mean-field boundary derived at
initialization need not describe trained states. We retained that boundary
without refitting it and treated gradient direction at a checkpoint and the
accuracy crossing after training as separate endpoints.

\subsection{Mean-field boundary for context-gated branch selection}

The Fashion-MNIST conflict experiment selects one branch $c$ uniformly from
$B$ branches. Only $\bm w_c^{\mathsf T}\bm x_c$ enters the logit, so the
exact derivative of every unselected branch is zero. Shared feedback omits
that selector and updates every active eligibility. For the centered
mean-field prediction, assume balanced class signs $s\in\{-1,1\}$,
class means $\E[\bm x\mid s]=s\bm v$, and zero logits, so the logistic
error is $\delta=-s/2$. An unselected input copies the selected image with
probability $1-\chi$ and is independently drawn from the opposite class
with probability $\chi$. Its mean class evidence relative to the selected
view is therefore $1-2\chi$. The resulting compatibility matrix is
\[
C_\chi=(1-2\chi)\bm 1\bm 1^{\mathsf T}+2\chi I.
\]
Its shared-mode eigenvalue is $B-2\chi(B-1)$, which vanishes at
$\chi_c=B/[2(B-1)]$. Under these population and initialization assumptions,
the expected shared update loses positive alignment with the exact update
at that boundary. Logistic derivatives and class-conditional moments need
not preserve this relation as weights change. The empirical trained
chance crossing is consequently a separate descriptive endpoint, not a
theorem about the entire optimization trajectory. The missing branch
selector may be supplied in feedback or in local eligibility; it is not
evidence that the unselected branches require opposite exact errors.

\subsection{Two-branch subtree-address experiment}

We first tested the prespecified within-neuron support contrast in a minimal
two-sibling model. The subsequent architecture-by-budget factorial varied the
number of feedback routes $K$. One pilot seed (4099), excluded from inference,
checked numerical integrity and nondegeneracy: neuron-shared capture
was 0.506, correct-route capture was 1.000, correct-route held-out accuracy was
0.814, and analytic exact path transport agreed identically with analytic
backpropagation. The same configuration was then used for ten paired analysis
seeds (4100--4109).

For seed-specific unit vectors $\bm u_A$ and $\bm u_B$, binary label sign
$y\in\{-1,1\}$ and context $c\in\{0,1\}$, the two input means were
\begin{align}
\mathbb E[\bm x_A\mid y,c]
&=y\,[\mathbb 1(c=0)-0.75\mathbb 1(c=1)]\bm u_A,\\
\mathbb E[\bm x_B\mid y,c]
&=y\,[\mathbb 1(c=1)-0.75\mathbb 1(c=0)]\bm u_B.
\label{eq:s_credit_reversal_task}
\end{align}
Each stream had 12 coordinates and independent Gaussian noise with standard
deviation 1.15. The 2,048-example train and test splits contained exactly 512
examples in each context--label cell. The somatic logit selected branch $A$
when $c=0$ and branch $B$ when $c=1$. Both inputs nevertheless remained
active, preventing the one-coordinate rule from acquiring branch specificity
through a zero eligibility on the distractor.

For somatic logit $z$ and logistic sigmoid $\sigma$, let
$\delta=\sigma(z)-\mathbb 1(y=1)$ and
$\bm r(c)=(\mathbb 1[c=0],\mathbb 1[c=1])$. Exact and correct-subtree
coefficients were $\delta\bm r(c)$; within-neuron derangement reversed the
two entries, giving $\delta\bm r(c)_{::-1}$; the shared neuronal and same-depth controls used
$\delta(1,1)$; and the random rank-two control used
$\delta\bm r(c)R_s^{\mathsf T}$ for a seed-fixed orthogonal rotation $R_s$.
The gated point emulation used one somatic coefficient together with the
context-gated features $(\mathbb 1[c=0]\bm x_A,
\mathbb 1[c=1]\bm x_B)$, making its alternative address resource explicit.
All conditions had 24 trainable weights and the same paired initialization and
batch order. The random dense control had four routing nonzeros; the sparse
two-route conditions had two.

Training used mini-batch stochastic gradient descent (SGD), learning rate 0.3,
batch size 64 and 60 epochs.
The switch endpoint applied another 12 epochs to 1,024 context-1 examples with
balanced labels and measured the change in context-0 accuracy. For gradient
geometry, exact and candidate per-example gradients were concatenated over
both branches. Scaled capture was squared cosine after optimal scalar
rescaling. The one-step candidate was normalized to the exact batch-gradient
norm and evaluated at step 0.25. Table~\ref{tab:subtree_phase1} reports every
tested condition; no condition was removed on the basis of its outcome.

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering
\caption{Two-branch within-neuron routing and context-switch results. Accuracy and forgetting are fractions; initial \(\cos^2\) measures directional agreement after optimal rescaling, as defined below the table. Values are means across ten independent paired analysis seeds; intervals for the principal differences are shown in Supplementary Fig.~S19A and reported in Source Data.}
\label{tab:subtree_phase1}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
Condition & Channels & Test accuracy & Initial $\cos^2$ & Switch forgetting \\
\midrule
Neuron-shared & 1 & 0.7624 & 0.5095 & 0.5566 \\
Same-depth neuron-shared & 1 & 0.7624 & 0.5095 & 0.5566 \\
Correct sibling subtrees & 2 & 0.8063 & 1.0000 & 0.0000 \\
Within-neuron derangement & 2 & 0.1963 & 0.0000 & 0.0006 \\
Random dense rank two & 2 & 0.5847 & 0.2791 & $-0.0014$ \\
Exact path transport & 2 & 0.8063 & 1.0000 & 0.0000 \\
Analytic backpropagation & 2 & 0.8063 & 1.0000 & 0.0000 \\
Gated point emulation & 1 + gated eligibility & 0.8063 & 1.0000 & 0.0000 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\vspace{3pt}
\footnotesize Accuracies and forgetting are fractions. Initial $\cos^2$
is the squared cosine between concatenated per-example exact and delivered
updates, equivalently capture after optimally rescaling that single update
direction; it is not capacity of the full route span and does not retain
orientation. Signed cosines are supplied in Source Data. Switch forgetting
is the loss of retained-context accuracy after context-specific retraining;
small values in the initially below-chance deranged condition reflect its
poor pre-switch solution. Gated eligibility supplies branch selection while
communicating one scalar error.
\end{table}

The very poor deranged loss and the variable random-map loss were retained as
prespecified negative controls. The equality of correct subtree routing, exact
transport, backpropagation and gated point emulation is equally important: in
this shallow model the exact path coefficient is only the binary branch gate.
The result therefore isolates address alignment but does not establish a
dendrite-specific advantage over a point system supplied with equivalent
gated parameter groups.

\subsection{Subtree-address bandwidth and architecture factorial}

To vary feedback bandwidth, the complete factorial extended the two-sibling
task to eight context-selected streams arranged in a balanced binary hierarchy. Context and binary
label were exactly balanced in train and test sets, every stream remained
active on every example, and the sign of the task signal reversed outside the
selected context. Thus a shared coefficient could not recover the required address simply
because off-route eligibilities were zero. The prespecified feedback budgets
were $K\in\{1,2,4,8\}$ nested routes. Twenty paired analysis seeds
(4200--4219) crossed five representations and 27 method--budget combinations,
giving 135 fits per seed and 2,700 fits in total.

The dendritic tree, explicitly gated point model, flat-compartment model and
grouped-point model were implementation-equivalent controls: each contained 64
trainable weights, 64 active input contacts and one output nonlinearity, and
each received the same routed coefficient field. The fifth representation was
a degree- and depth-matched rewired tree. Its resource counts were unchanged,
but its ancestry groups were permuted relative to the task hierarchy. Feedback
families comprised correct ancestry, fixed within-neuron derangement,
depth-interleaved groups, random sparse groups, a seed-fixed random rank-$K$
map, and a task-fitted rank-$K$ upper bound. Neuron-shared, exact-transport and
analytic-backpropagation references completed the design. Route fields were
unit-normalized per example. Data, initialization and every
stochastic control map were keyed to seed, method family and $K$, so matched
representations saw identical stochastic realizations.

Initial pilot checks verified exact context balance, zero context--label
correlation, nonzero activity in all streams, neuron-shared gradient capture
below 0.2, exact capture of one, numerical equality of analytic exact path transport
and backpropagation, and zero fallback counts. After the first large run, we
found that stochastic control maps had not been paired across representation
labels. Those outcomes were excluded before scientific analysis. We corrected
the random streams, added cross-representation implementation equivalence to
the pilot checks and repeated the complete 2,700-fit cohort. Only the corrected
cohort is reported.

Table~\ref{tab:subtree_factorial} gives the primary bandwidth contrasts.
Correct nested-subtree routing strongly exceeded route derangement at $K=2$ and $K=4$, but
its comparison with the best matched non-anatomical control was deliberately
more stringent: ancestry lost at $K=1$ and $K=2$, won by 0.0127 accuracy at
$K=4$ (95\% paired seed-bootstrap interval 0.00593--0.01946; 15/20 seeds;
paired Wilcoxon signed-rank Holm-adjusted $P=0.0101$ across the four budgets;
Table~S2 retains the paired mean-sign-flip sensitivity test), and tied at full rank. Correct routing on the
task-matched tree exceeded the rewired tree at $K=2$ and $K=4$ in all 20
seeds. The four matched representations were exactly equal in every condition
(maximum held-out-accuracy difference zero). The experiment therefore supports
an intermediate-bandwidth advantage for a task-aligned nested address, while
showing that an explicitly routed point implementation can reproduce it.

\subsection{Trained partition-residual reconstruction}

Using the saved configurations and outputs, we quantified irreducible address
error in a post hoc diagnostic of the complete factorial. For example $b$, let
$c_b$ denote context, $\delta_b$ the somatic error, $e_{c_b,i}$ the indicator
selecting leaf $i$, and $\bm x_{bi}$ its input vector. We defined the exact
coefficient field $c_{bi}=\delta_b e_{c_b,i}$, coordinate weight
$w_{bi}=\lVert\bm x_{bi}\rVert_2^2$, and active route field $\phi_{bi}$.
Allowing the best single coefficient for that route on each example gives
\begin{equation}
 z_b^*=\frac{\sum_i w_{bi}\phi_{bi}c_{bi}}
 {\sum_iw_{bi}\phi_{bi}^2},\qquad
 \mathcal C_{\mathrm{part}}=1-
 \frac{\sum_{b,i}w_{bi}(c_{bi}-z_b^*\phi_{bi})^2}
 {\sum_{b,i}w_{bi}c_{bi}^2}.
\label{eq:s_trained_partition_capture}
\end{equation}
This is the normalized complement of the shared-coefficient residual in
Eq.~\ref{eq:s_partition_variance}. It grants an optimal route coefficient and
therefore isolates address/support error from the coefficient scale actually
used during training.

All 20 seeds and 135 conditions per seed were reconstructed at initialization
and after 80 training epochs. Reconstructed losses agreed with the recorded
values within $4.97\times10^{-9}$ and accuracies within $5.01\times10^{-11}$,
the precision of the saved decimal tables; the address-plus-coefficient Pythagorean
decomposition closed within $7.28\times10^{-12}$. On the dendritic-tree
representation, trained correct-ancestry capture was 0.162, 0.315, 0.547 and
1.000 at $K=1,2,4,8$. Fixed coordinate-to-arbor derangement had the common scalar value
at $K=1$ and zero capture at $K=2,4,8$, because its active support excluded the
target leaf (Supplementary Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_partition_residual}A).

For each seed we averaged implementation-equivalent representations within
feedback family and budget, then correlated trained capture with held-out
accuracy across conditions. The mean within-seed Spearman correlation was
0.824 (95\% seed-bootstrap interval 0.810--0.838; 20/20 positive; two-sided
Wilcoxon $P=1.91\times10^{-6}$). Restricting the analysis to the six route
families and $K<8$ removed the exact full-rank boundary and gave 0.639
(0.610--0.665; 20/20 positive; $P=1.91\times10^{-6}$; Supplementary
Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_partition_residual}B,C). This association is descriptive
and post hoc. In particular, ancestry, depth-interleaved and random sparse
partitions had similar address capture at matched $K$, so the residual does
not explain the small trained ancestry advantage at $K=4$.

\subsection{Learning coefficients from an explicit local context cue}
\label{note:local_context_encoder}

To test whether coefficients could be learned from a supplied cue, we used a
four-route dictionary on fresh versions of the balanced eight-context task.
We retained its 64 forward weights, paired initialization,
2,048-example train and test sets, and 80 full-batch task-training updates.
The dictionary was fixed before task training:
$\Phi_{bk}=\mathbf{1}[k=\lfloor b/2\rfloor]/\sqrt2$ for zero-indexed
blocks $b=0,\ldots,7$ and routes $k=0,\ldots,3$.
For trial $i$ with context $c_i$, the supplied local cue was
$\bm h_i=\bm e_{c_i}+\sigma_h\bm\epsilon_i$, with
$\bm\epsilon_i\sim\mathcal N(0,I_8)$. Here $\bm e_{c_i}$ is the one-hot
context vector and $\sigma_h$ is cue-noise SD. The cue is an additional
information resource; the experiment does not infer a biological signal.

Separate balanced calibration trials supplied the target route-activation
vector $\bm e_{\lfloor c_i/2\rfloor}$. A four-output softmax encoder with
an intercept learned this mapping by minibatch delta-rule updates. Weights
started at zero; calibration used 30 epochs, batch size 16, step 0.2 and
weight decay 0.001 on non-bias weights. Calibration sets of 16, 64 and 256
trials were nested within each cue-noise condition. Because epoch count was
fixed, increasing sample count also increased the number of encoder updates.
This axis therefore changes both calibration data and computation.
No task labels, exact task gradients or held-out task outcomes entered encoder
training. Its parameters were frozen before the classification weights were
trained.

At cue delays $d=0,1,4$ trials, the encoder received the cue array circularly
shifted by $d$. Predicted coefficients $\bm c_i$ produced the normalized
field $\Phi\bm c_i/\|\Phi\bm c_i\|$. Delay is a mismatch between the
current eligibility and an earlier independent context; it is not a calibrated
physiological time constant. Controls used exact one-hot route selection,
a uniform frozen profile, or a cyclic permutation of the learned coefficient
coordinates. Exact and frozen controls repeated across nuisance conditions
are algebraic repetitions rather than independent evidence. Task updates used
the original factor-eight context rescaling and step 0.3.

Twenty analysis seeds (52000--52019) followed the excluded implementation-check
seed 51999.
Crossing three calibration sizes, cue-noise SDs $0,0.5,1$, delays and four
rules produced 2,160 task fits and 10,800 checkpoint rows. At task epochs
0, 1, 10, 40 and 80, we recorded held-out accuracy/loss and delivered-versus-
exact gradient cosine at each method's own state. Held-out cue classification,
coefficient mean squared error (MSE) and route-norm errors supplied separate
encoder diagnostics.
The protocol, fixed before new outcomes but not publicly preregistered, is
\texttt{configs/review\_completion/coefficient\_encoder.json}.

The primary condition used 256 calibration trials, cue-noise SD 0.5 and no
delay. Learned coefficients improved accuracy over the frozen profile by
7.16 percentage points (95\% paired-seed bootstrap interval 6.26--8.07;
20/20 positive) and over mismatched coefficients by 7.10 points
(6.19--7.99; 20/20). They remained 54.47 points below exact route selection
(53.54--55.45; 20/20 lower). Two-sided Wilcoxon tests restored the exact
$1/2048$ accuracy-count grid before ranking and used Holm correction across
these three primary contrasts. Adjusted $P$ values were
$5.72\times10^{-6}$ versus the frozen rule and
$1.77\times10^{-4}$ versus each other control. Intervals used 20,000
paired-seed draws; other grid contrasts are descriptive sensitivities.
All fits were finite. Learning from the supplied cue improved performance
relative to fixed or misassigned coefficients, but left a large gap to the
oracle. The experiment does not establish autonomous discovery of credit
coordinates in a biological neuron. The complete outputs are in
\texttt{source\_data/review\_coefficient\_encoder/}.


An exploratory follow-up was specified after observing the soft-encoder
outcomes. The same trained encoder, original paired seeds and task data were
retained, but its maximum-probability route was delivered as a one-hot vector.
No encoder parameter was refitted and no hyperparameter was selected from
the task outcomes. This hard readout is a paired sensitivity, not a new
independent replication or a replacement for the primary soft rule
(Supplementary Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_local_context_encoder}). At the primary
noise SD 0.5, mean accuracy was 25.825\% for soft coefficients, 64.031\%
for hard selection, 80.291\% for exact selection and 18.669\% for the
frozen profile. Hard-minus-oracle accuracy remained $-16.26$ percentage
points (95\% paired-seed interval $-17.83$ to $-14.83$), while hard-minus-
frozen was 45.36 points (43.69--46.92). At zero noise and 256 calibration
trials, hard selection equaled the oracle numerically, whereas the soft rule
reached 79.978\%. Both learned readouts failed when cues were delayed across
independent contexts. Thus accurate cue identification does not guarantee
that graded coefficients are suitable for the learning rule: readout and
timing are separate constraints. All exploratory fits and unchanged encoder
settings are in \texttt{source\_data/review\_coefficient\_hard\_readout/}.

\subsection{Nonlinear physical depth in the DendriNet model}

Changing the resolution of a linear route projector does not change the
physical depth of a forward computation. We therefore tested physical depth
separately in the nonlinear DendriNet population model. The
\texttt{hierarchical\_gain\_load} generator emits 64 strictly positive
excitatory rates followed by 64 strictly positive inhibitory rates. Three
independent zero-mean Gaussian node fields define fine, coarse and global
lognormal gain factors, giving physical task hierarchy $H_{\mathrm p}=3$ (H3). Independent Gaussian rate noise is added to each
baseline before clipping the rate to a floor of $10^{-4}$; gains are applied
after this clipping. The factor product multiplies signal-bearing excitatory
coordinates in the distal block; the three inhibitory blocks separately
measure the corresponding factors. Lognormal means are corrected by
$-\sigma^2/2$ at every factor, so changing hierarchy or sensor alignment does
not silently change the means of the multiplicative factors. The additive
noise before clipping is zero mean; the resulting rate perturbation is not.
This distinction matters in middle and proximal excitatory blocks, whose
class-independent baseline is 0.02 and noise standard deviation is 0.08.

The matched condition uses the same latent node in excitatory signal and its
inhibitory sensor. In the zero-alignment condition, each sensor instead uses
an independent node field with the same scale and grouping. In the shuffled
condition, a nonzero cyclic trial shift is applied to the complete inhibitory
field after class balancing. The tree-placement condition leaves every data
tensor unchanged and reverses the fine and global feature ranges across the
fine/coarse/proximal inventory tiers. These controls remove task--tree
alignment in different ways: latent correspondence, example-wise access and
physical placement, respectively. The final placement control reverses the
fine and global ranges and preserves their 21/22/21 widths. An initial
three-cycle placement changed the number of candidate mask slots and therefore
violated the predefined resource-matching criterion. It was excluded before
inference and is reported only as an excluded design check.

In DendriNet, branch factors are ordered from soma to distal dendrite and
$D_{\mathrm p}$ denotes the number of physical dendritic stages.
For architecture $[b_1,\ldots,b_{D_{\mathrm{p}}}]$, the number of nonsomatic
units is $\sum_{j=1}^{D_{\mathrm{p}}}\prod_{r=1}^{j}b_r$. Thus D1 $[8]$ has
eight distal units per soma; D2 $[2,3]$ has two proximal and six distal units;
and D3 $[2,1,2]$ has two proximal, two middle and four distal units.
The unary middle expansion is deliberate: all depths have eight nonsomatic
units. The structured masks assign all 4/2/2 tiers to the
single D1 stage, fine+coarse tiers to the D2 distal stage and the global tier
to its proximal stage, and one fine, coarse or global tier to each D3 stage.
The architectures were matched for
trainable parameters (66,178), active synapses (14,336), candidate slots
(21,760) and persistent state scalars per sample (2,944). The physical branch
law was the same positive-conductance division used elsewhere in the paper,
with identity reactivation and learned softplus-positive synaptic weights.

The exploratory calibration used signal contrast 0.24, training gain SD 0.25 and an
unseen test gain SD 1.2. Deeper networks fit training better, but BP and both
local credit assignment (LocalCA) transports remained at chance on the shifted
test distribution
(Supplementary Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_nonlinear_depth_calibration}A). This condition
did not meet the predefined accessibility criterion and is reported as a
negative calibration result. A two-seed test-gain sweep showed monotone
erosion under increasing shift. A coupling sweep
at matched train/test gain identified a seed-consistent interaction: from
child conductance 1 to 64, the D3-minus-D1 accuracy contrast increased by
0.1236 on average, while D1 changed minimally. A signal-contrast sweep at child
conductance 16 then located the non-ceiling signal-contrast boundary. Contrast
0.72 narrowly missed the predefined D1 accessibility threshold (mean 0.597),
whereas contrast 0.80 met it, with D1, D2 and D3 means 0.6086,
0.6737 and 0.9227. These exploratory seeds 10100--10141 were excluded from
inference.

Before observing any outcome for seeds 10200--10209, we prespecified a 270-fit
analysis at signal contrast 0.80, per-factor train/test log-gain SD 0.25 and
child conductance 16. The
nine feedback--placement conditions were aligned shunting BP; zero-alignment, shuffled-sensor and
reversed-placement shunting BP; aligned raw-additive BP; and aligned or
reversed-placement shunting LocalCA with shared-soma and exact-path feedback. Each
condition crossed D1, D2 and D3 over the same ten paired seeds 10200--10209.
We specified the primary contrasts, resource- and accessibility-matching
criteria, bootstrap procedure and limits on interpretation in advance. In particular, aligned D3--D1 alone
tests forward capacity. Task--tree specificity requires an
aligned-minus-control depth interaction, and a local-credit claim requires
the corresponding LocalCA contrast. The raw-additive tree is a forward-mechanism control; a
point model with sufficient flexible capacity can in principle emulate the
hierarchy, a boundary tested directly in the same-task controls below.

The accessibility criteria required at least nine of ten aligned-BP seeds
above 0.55 at each depth and every condition mean below 0.98. All 270 fits
met the completeness, finite-metric, no-fallback and exact-resource criteria. With aligned shunting BP, mean accuracy was 0.6096,
0.6752 and 0.9182 at D1--D3, giving a paired D3-minus-D1 effect of 30.86
percentage points (95\% seed-bootstrap interval 30.45--31.30; 10/10 positive;
exact sign-flip $P=0.001953$). The raw depth effects were 0.02 points for
independent sensors, $-0.01$ for shuffled trials and $-0.43$ for reversed
placement. Their aligned-minus-control depth interactions were 30.84, 30.87
and 31.28 points, respectively, all positive in 10/10 paired seeds. Aligned
raw-additive BP instead fell from 0.5733 to 0.5190. Under LocalCA, aligned
D3-minus-D1 was 16.95 points (16.37--17.44) for shared-soma LocalCA and 27.90
points (27.24--28.53) for exact-path LocalCA. The corresponding
aligned-minus-reversed interactions were 17.72 points (17.27--18.18) and
28.68 points (28.07--29.27), both positive in 10/10 seeds. These interactions,
rather than the aligned depth effects alone, support task--tree specificity.

In a post hoc mechanism analysis, we evaluated one fixed held-out batch at
each of the 30 aligned-shunting BP checkpoints. Exact-path LocalCA reproduced the selected conductance-gradient direction
computed by automatic differentiation (autograd)
at every checkpoint (minimum cosine 0.9999999999994; no missing gradient
tensors). Mean shared-soma cosine was 0.9457 at D1, 0.7990 at D2 and 0.7674 at
D3. Its paired D3-minus-D1 change was $-0.1784$ (95\% seed-bootstrap interval
$-0.2082$ to $-0.1490$; 0/10 positive pairs). This analysis verifies the
path recursion and shows that a common neuronal error becomes a
poorer directional proxy as additional physical stages differentiate branch
states. It was not prespecified as a performance endpoint.

In Supplementary Fig.~\ref{fig:physicaldepth}D, an aligned row maximum is
outlined only when its 95\% bootstrap interval clears the runner-up's;
reversed rows are unoutlined. Panel E outlines descriptive maxima only when
the within-row spread exceeds one percentage point; blank cells were untested.

\subsection{Same-task point, nonserial and credit-coordinate controls}

We added three controls without changing the calibrated task, data, seeds,
initialization policy or stopping rule. First, an all-active grouped-star model
used exactly the same branch layers, structured masks and parameters as the
serial tree, but each nonsomatic layer read the external input independently;
the child-aggregation parameters were preserved for resource matching but
summed only once at the soma. Stored model states and resource counts were identical at initialization. D1 logits were
numerically identical, as required. At D1 the serial-minus-star contrast was
$-0.005$ percentage points ($-0.067$ to 0.054; 6/10 positive; $P=0.844$).
At D2 and D3 it was 6.54 (6.20--6.88) and 30.81 points (30.41--31.26),
respectively, both positive in 10/10 seeds ($P=0.001953$). Under reversed
placement the D3 serial-minus-star contrast was $-0.43$ points ($-0.68$ to
$-0.16$; 1/10 positive; $P=0.0137$), and the paired alignment interaction was
31.24 points (30.65--31.77; 10/10 positive; $P=0.001953$). Serial divisive
composition, rather than the inventory of branch units, is therefore required
for the aligned effect in this model.

Second, point multilayer perceptron (MLP) widths were chosen before fitting
to match either 15,055
active parameters or, to the nearest realizable integer width, 66,200 total
parameters (the tree has 66,178). Their mean test accuracies were 0.9861 and
0.9902. They exceeded serial D3 by 6.79 points (6.19--7.34) and 7.20 points
(6.75--7.68), respectively, in 10/10 paired seeds ($P=0.001953$ for each).
Neither point model was given the hierarchy. These results rule out an
absolute expressivity advantage for the tree on this task, while retaining
the narrower interpretation from the controls with matched branch resources.

Third, soma-broadcast autograd used the standard synaptic derivatives but
replaced each nonsomatic loss derivative by the loss derivative at its owning
soma. Forward values and logits were identical to full BP before training.
With the standard BP optimizer, full BP minus soma broadcast at D3 was 0.64
points (0.15--1.17; 8/10 positive; $P=0.0332$). After this 140-fit analysis was
complete, we found that the LocalCA reference used a different, split
optimizer. We therefore specified 60 additional soma-broadcast fits using the
LocalCA optimizer before viewing their outcomes. This optimizer-matched
broadcast reached 0.7771 at aligned D3, compared
with 0.7778 for shared-soma LocalCA: soma-broadcast autograd minus shared-soma
LocalCA was $-0.06$ points
($-0.23$ to 0.09; 5/10 positive; $P=0.492$). Changing only the broadcast
optimizer cost 13.47 points (12.89--14.05; 10/10 positive). Within LocalCA,
exact-path LocalCA exceeded shared-soma LocalCA by 10.96 points (10.34--11.58;
10/10 positive), while full BP remained 3.09 points above exact-path LocalCA
(2.48--3.79; 10/10 positive). After matching the teaching coordinate and optimizer, we detected no
performance penalty from the tested eligibility expression on this task. The 10.96-point path-specificity contrast is conditional on the
split LocalCA optimizer; it is not an optimizer-independent requirement of
three serial stages. The corresponding full-BP versus soma-broadcast
contrast under the standard optimizer was only 0.64 points. The optimizer-matched comparison is a post hoc diagnostic rather than an
independent confirmation. Automatic differentiation serves as an
implementation control; it does not specify a biological mechanism.

Across these controls, all 200 fits had finite outcomes, contained no fallback
warning and used exactly seeds 10200--10209. Serial and star models
had 66,178 trainable parameters, 14,336 active contacts, 21,760 candidate slots
and 2,944 persistent state scalars. Seed-level outcomes, paired contrasts,
model configurations and machine-readable validation records are provided in
\texttt{source\_data/point\_dendrite\_credit\_controls/}.

\input{physical_depth_budget_methods.tex}

\subsection{Alignment-dose interpolation of the physical-depth effect}

We interpolated sensor alignment $\alpha$ after observing the $\alpha=0$ and
$1$ endpoint results. Before viewing any intermediate outcome, we fixed
$\alpha\in\{0.25,0.50,0.75\}$ and left the generator, architecture, optimizer,
stopping and seed settings unchanged. The 90 fits crossed three physical
depths with ten paired seeds. Combined with the
endpoints, paired D3-minus-D1 effects were 0.019, 0.207, 0.820, 3.126 and
30.855 percentage points at $\alpha=0,0.25,0.50,0.75,1$, respectively. The
corresponding positive-pair counts were 6, 8, 10, 10 and 10 of ten. Exact
two-sided sign-flip tests gave $P=0.844$ at $\alpha=0$, 0.0703 at $0.25$ and
0.00195 at every higher level.

The predefined summary fit an ordinary least-squares line to the five
paired depth effects within each seed. Its mean slope was 25.84 percentage
points per unit $\alpha$ (95\% paired-seed bootstrap interval 25.43--26.26;
10/10 positive; exact sign-flip $P=0.001953$). The fitted slope summarizes the increase but does not establish a linear
response. The change from $\alpha=0.25$ to $0.75$ was only
2.92 points (2.65--3.17), whereas the final $0.75$ to $1$ step contributed
27.73 points (27.35--28.11). The interpolation therefore supports a steep
boundary near complete alignment in this calibrated task. It does not supply
a second task family, hierarchy depth or biological replication.

The 90-fit dataset contains results obtained immediately before and after an
unrelated source-code edit. Direct comparison showed that the edit introduced
only an optional rule override that none of these model configurations used;
condition-level deduplication yielded one result per condition. All 90 unique
intermediate fits met the finite-metric, no-fallback, seed and equal-resource
criteria. Software-version records, machine-readable outcomes and paired
contrasts are in
\texttt{source\_data/physical\_alignment\_dose/}.

\subsection{Literal grouped-point control and independent hierarchy depth}

The all-active grouped star retained the serial model's child-aggregation
modules in an arrangement of independent branches. To remove that residual
architectural feature, we implemented a literal grouped-point control: each
nonsomatic level received the raw external excitatory and inhibitory streams
independently and projected directly to its owning soma.
Every local branch module, structured mask and shunting equation is preserved,
but no level inherits another branch state. Each direct projection has the
same scalar count as the serial child aggregator it replaces and copies its
initialized raw conductances. We verified the copied projection parameters,
finite gradients and exact D1 logits against the serial hierarchy.

This control was designed after the preceding $H_{\mathrm p}=3$ endpoints were
known. Its 60 literal grouped-point fits and comparisons were specified before
their outcomes were viewed; they crossed aligned or reversed placement, D1--D3
and the same paired seeds 10200--10209. The previously reported serial outcomes
served as the fixed reference. Under alignment, grouped-point means were 0.6096,
0.6100 and 0.6095 at D1--D3. Serial-minus-grouped-point was 30.87 percentage
points at D3 (95\% paired-seed bootstrap interval 30.48--31.31; 10/10
positive; exact sign-flip $P=0.001953$), but $-0.44$ points after placement
reversal ($-0.69$ to $-0.16$). The resulting alignment interaction was 31.31
points (30.73--31.84; 10/10 positive). Grouped star minus the
literal grouped point was 0.06 points at aligned D3 (0.01--0.13; 7/10
positive; $P=0.0840$), which did not meet the predefined directional criterion.

We prospectively tested a second hierarchy depth, fixing its design before
observing outcomes. The $H_{\mathrm p}=2$ (H2) generator used two 32-feature sensor
tiers, fixed inventory counts 4/4
and exact-resource D1 $[8]$ and D2 $[4,1]$ morphologies. All remaining task,
signal, gain, axial-coupling, optimizer and stopping settings were inherited
without retuning. Aligned and reversed placement were crossed with serial BP,
literal grouped-point BP and serial LocalCA with shared-soma or exact-path
transport over ten paired seeds (10300--10309), yielding 160 fits.

Aligned serial BP rose from 0.6588 at D1 to 0.9672 at D2. The 30.84-point
depth effect (30.46--31.23; 10/10 positive; $P=0.001953$) became $-1.56$
points under reversed placement, giving a 32.40-point interaction
(32.10--32.70). Grouped-point depth effects were 0.02 points under alignment
($-0.02$ to 0.06) and $-0.02$ under reversal ($-0.06$ to 0.02); their
alignment interaction was 0.04 points ($-0.03$ to 0.11). Serial-minus-grouped
at aligned D2 was 30.82 points (30.41--31.23), and its architecture-by-
placement interaction was 32.36 points (32.02--32.71). Shared-soma LocalCA
showed a 21.20-point aligned depth effect (19.34--23.00) and a 22.93-point
placement interaction (21.02--24.77). Exact-path LocalCA showed 31.38 points
(30.95--31.83) and a 33.36-point interaction (33.00--33.75). Every positive
interaction was ordered in 10/10 seeds with exact sign-flip $P=0.001953$.

All 220 fits were finite and stage complete, with no fallback or nonfinite
values, the expected seeds and exact within-hierarchy resource equality. The
second hierarchy is a replication within the same synthetic mechanism-matched
task family, not an independent dataset. Model configurations, software
versions, per-seed metrics and every paired contrast are in
\texttt{source\_data/remaining\_physical\_experiments/}.

\subsection{Four-level hierarchy and computational reproducibility}

The four-level hierarchy (H4) used the same positive-rate generator, optimizer,
conductance scale, stopping rule and calibrated signal regime without
retuning. Its four 16-feature signal/sensor tiers had predefined inventory
counts
$[4,2,1,1]$. D1 $[8]$, D2 $[2,3]$, D3 $[2,1,2]$ and D4 $[1,1,2,2]$ each
contained eight nonsomatic branch units per soma, 66,178 trainable parameters,
14,336 active contacts, 21,760 candidate slots and 2,944 persistent state
scalars. The 360 planned fits crossed aligned and reversed placement for
serial and literal grouped-point BP and for shared-soma and exact-path LocalCA;
aligned serial raw-additive BP supplied the mechanism control. Ten
paired seeds (10400--10409) were the inferential units.

The primary comparisons were prespecified before outcomes were analyzed:
aligned serial-BP D4--D3 and
D4--D1, their placement interactions, the D4 architecture-by-placement
interaction, shared-soma and exact-path LocalCA D4--D3 effects and interactions, and the
shunting-minus-raw-additive D4--D3 interaction. Failure of D4 to exceed D3 was
defined prospectively as falsification of the simple $D_{\mathrm p}=H_{\mathrm p}$
trained crossover. Intervals used 50,000 paired-seed bootstrap draws, exact
two-sided sign-flip tests enumerated all $2^{10}$ assignments, and the primary
family used Benjamini--Hochberg (BH) correction, with adjusted $P$ values
denoted by $q$. A directional positive statement
also required at least eight of ten paired differences in the predicted
direction.

The initial D3 configurations did not instantiate because
the automatic tier partition grouped the H4 inventory as $[4],[2+1],[1]$
rather than the intended D3 row counts $[4,2,2]$. No H4 outcome was inspected
before this construction error was diagnosed. After 33 targeted construction
tests, the explicit D3 grouping was defined as $[4],[2],[1+1]$ without examining
H4 outcomes. The default automatic grouping was unchanged. Only the 90 failed
D3 configurations were repeated, using the same seeds and scientific settings.
The Source Data identify the software version and result origin for each row.
All 360 planned seed--condition outcomes were then
finite, stage complete and resource matched, with no fallback warning.

Aligned serial-BP means were 0.5963, 0.7112, 0.8671 and 0.8525 at D1--D4.
D4 minus D3 was $-1.46$ percentage points (95\% paired-seed bootstrap interval
$-1.74$ to $-1.16$; 0/10 positive; exact $P=0.001953$; BH $q=0.003906$),
whereas D4 minus D1 was 25.63 points (24.71--26.40; 10/10 positive;
$q=0.003906$). The D4--D3 placement interaction was $-0.36$ points
($-0.70$ to 0.04), but the D4--D1 placement interaction was 25.94 points
(25.06--26.66; 10/10 positive). Thus aligned serial depth remained useful
relative to D1 but saturated at D3, falsifying one-to-one tracking at H4.

Grouped-point BP stayed near 0.596 across depths. Serial minus grouped point at
aligned D4 was 25.60 points (24.69--26.37; 10/10 positive), and the
architecture-by-placement interaction was 25.91 points (25.06--26.62).
Shared-soma LocalCA gained 5.27 points from D3 to D4 (4.84--5.72; 10/10), but
exact-path LocalCA gained 0.60 points (0.04--1.25; 7/10; $P=0.0859$) and did
not meet the predefined positive-direction criterion. The aligned raw-additive
D4--D3 effect was
$-0.55$ points ($-2.24$ to 1.12), and its interaction with shunting was
$-0.90$ points ($-2.65$ to 0.95). All null and reversed contrasts are reported
in \texttt{source\_data/physical\_depth\_h4\_factorial/}, irrespective of
direction.

We separately tested computational reproducibility by repeating all 220
H2/literal-point fits and 210 principal H3 fits with versioned source
commit \texttt{a99c3a7}, using the same configurations and paired seeds. All 430 repeated fits met the
source-version, completeness, finite-metric, seed and resource criteria. The
median absolute original-versus-repeated difference in held-out accuracy
was 0.030 percentage points, the mean difference was 0.154 points, and 357/430
pairs were within 0.1 points. The maximum difference was 15.01 points in the
raw-additive forward-mechanism control. Rerun H2 and H3 aligned
serial-BP depth effects were 30.84 and 30.86 points; the
architecture-by-placement interactions were 32.43 and 31.27 points. Every
principal shunting and local-credit comparison preserved its direction and met
the corresponding predefined criterion in both analyses.
This is a same-seed computational reproducibility test, not independent task or
biological replication (Supplementary Fig.~\ref{fig:supp_clean_source}).

\subsection{Fixed-depth task-family and alignment factorial}

To test whether the depth benefit depended on the task's factorization, we
fixed the DendriNet architecture at D3 $[2,1,2]$ and crossed three data
generators, three task--sensor alignments
$\alpha\in\{0,0.5,1\}$, serial versus literal grouped-point composition,
and BP versus exact-path LocalCA. Ten paired seeds 10500--10509
gave 360 planned fits. All architectures used 66,178 trainable
parameters, 14,336 active contacts, 21,760 candidate slots, 2,944 persistent
state scalars and identical input tensors. Only child-to-parent composition
differed between the serial and grouped-point models.

For the nested-factor family, independent, identically distributed latent
log-gain variables $z_1,z_2,z_3\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal N(0,1)$ modulated the
signal-bearing excitatory coordinate by
\begin{equation}
 x_E=\max\{b_E+y\Delta+\epsilon_E,10^{-4}\}
 \exp\!\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}
 \sigma_\ell z_\ell-\frac12\sum_{\ell=1}^{3}\sigma_\ell^2\right),
 \label{eq:s_task_family_nested}
\end{equation}
where $b_E$ is the excitatory baseline, $y\in\{0,1\}$ is the balanced class
label, $\Delta=0.80$ is the class-signal contrast,
$\sigma_\ell=0.25$ is the level-wise log-gain standard deviation, and
$\epsilon_E$ is Gaussian noise with standard deviation 0.08 before the
positive-rate floor is applied. The same order---add noise, clip, then
multiply by gains---is used for inhibitory sensor rates. Clipping makes the
effective rate noise nonzero-mean, particularly in the class-independent
middle and proximal excitatory blocks with baseline 0.02. Sensor
block $\ell$ observed
\begin{equation}
 s_\ell(\alpha)=\alpha z_\ell+\sqrt{1-\alpha^2}\,\xi_\ell,
 \qquad \xi_\ell\stackrel{\mathrm{iid}}{\sim}\mathcal N(0,1).
 \label{eq:s_task_family_alignment}
\end{equation}
Here the sensor-noise variables $\xi_\ell$ are independent standard
Gaussians, independent of the latent gains. The interpolation preserves unit
sensor variance while changing how accurately a sensor reports the
trial-specific gain that obscures the class signal.

The flat-factor control preserved the factor product and the same level-wise
marginal variances. All three factors used the same deterministic eight-bin
spatial partition, with independently sampled amplitudes for each factor;
there was no fine-to-global nesting of supports. The
local-ratio control multiplied excitation and inhibition at each level by
correlated cumulative hierarchical fields. It therefore provided local
divisive information rather than requiring serial cancellation of separate
factors. Every family contained 10,000 balanced examples with 64 excitatory
and 64 inhibitory features; the class signal remained distal-only.

The primary estimands were paired serial-minus-grouped differences and the
