Teacher-task permutations preserve the input-gradient spectrum. These are planted mechanistic controls within the stated model class. \textbf{B}, Analytic population lower bound from interactions crossing the student's two proximal input blocks, evaluated by converged quadrature. Dots average the six incompatible task/grouping pairs within each of twenty seeds; the mean bound is 0.013222 population normalized mean squared error (NMSE). This is neither an observed loss nor a certified numerical integration bound. \textbf{C,D}, Final held-out sample NMSE for exact path, fixed calibrated broadcast, one oracle profile and two subtree-profile credit rules under Adam and stochastic gradient descent (SGD), respectively. Circles average three compatible task permutations within seed; open squares also average the two incompatible groupings per task. Rates were selected on development seeds and frozen before fresh outcomes. The fixed broadcast profile is calibrated from initial student path sensitivities; projection coefficients access the current exact field and are oracle controls. \textbf{E}, Paired compatible-tree fixed-broadcast-minus-exact NMSE differences. The Adam interval includes zero; the small SGD effect does not establish a universal need for precise credit. Dots are the twenty independent seed means. Means and whiskers in \textbf{B--E} use 10,000 whole-seed bootstrap draws and pointwise 95\% intervals. Population-normalized bounds in \textbf{B} and split-variance-normalized test errors in \textbf{C--E} have distinct denominators. \textbf{F}, Mean aggregate gradient cosine on 256 calibration examples for compatible Adam models, evaluated at common exact-learning checkpoints; no uncertainty band is shown. \begin{figure}[p]

\caption{\textbf{Boolean interactions distinguish structure, depth and canonical credit.} \textbf{A}, Complete truth tables of seven four-input templates, with sixteen equally weighted patterns (white, 0; green, 1). Mixed targets are \((a\land b)\lor(c\land d)\), \((a\land b)\mathbin{\mathrm{XOR}}(c\land d)\), \((a\mathbin{\mathrm{XOR}}b)\land(c\mathbin{\mathrm{XOR}}d)\), and nested \(a\land[b\lor(c\land d)]\). \textbf{B}, Centered-cut normalized-mean-squared-error (NMSE) regression lower bounds for all fifteen labeled binary trees. Green dots mark 49 exactly representable family--tree pairs, independently certified with coefficients in $[-2,2]$. The largest coefficient magnitude in these constructions is $4/\sqrt{15}$. All trees have twelve coefficients and six edges; T identifiers and cut sets are supplied in Source Data. A positive bound concerns truth-value regression, not threshold classification. \textbf{C}, OR-of-AND and the nested function each use two AND gates and one OR gate, but require minimum exact depths two and three. \textbf{D}, Minimum exact depth and compatible-tree counts. AND, OR and parity admit all fifteen trees; each mixed-gate target admits one. All seven input-sensitivity matrices have rank four, but their spectra generally differ. \textbf{E}, Explicitly post hoc target-projection energy $\|\mathbb E[z\mid x_S]\|^2$ for all six two-input subsets, with normalized target $z$. Parity has zero energy on every proper subset; XOR-of-AND has energy $1/5$ on every pair, including crossed pairs. This describes local target information and does not predict grouping compatibility or prove broadcast-learning failure. \textbf{F}, Canonical derivatives \(\partial F/\partial u\) with respect to left-branch output \(u\), versus right-branch output \(v\): AND, \(v\); OR, \(1-v\); XOR, \(1-2v\). These raw Boolean coordinates are illustrative; training can change every coefficient and need not preserve them. No learning outcome is inferred from a derivative sign alone. Panels are exhaustive analytic diagnostics, with no sampling error bars; the multi-affine and independent-input assumptions are explicit in Supplementary Note~\ref{note:boolean_morphology}.}

The general bound instead uses the delivered update's mean and covariance (Supplementary Section~S4); utility is zero for nonpositive descent alignment. \textbf{B}, Eight-terminal illustration of signal hierarchy \(H_{\rm c}\) and feedback resolution \(D_{\rm r}\). Quantitative screens use 16 Haar coordinates and no forward neuron. \textbf{C}, Subtree-minus-random capture over budget and covariance-mixture coefficient \(\rho\). Endpoints are isospectral; intermediate mixtures also change concentration. The full-rank contrast is zero. \textbf{D}, Final quadratic loss; amplified fine-scale noise makes matched signal and route resolution the designed optimum. \textbf{E}, Analytic projected-minus-unprojected one-step loss, \((f_{\rm noise}-f_{\rm sig})/2\), where \(f_{\rm sig}\) and \(f_{\rm noise}\) are retained signal and noise fractions under the stated normalization. Nonpositive alignment gives zero utility in 214 fits. Panels \textbf{C,D,F}: 50 paired seeds; lines/ribbons in \textbf{D,F} follow the specified analytic construction. Panel \textbf{G}: 5,000 bootstrap draws over 20 seed blocks.}

\section{Statistical analysis, reproducibility and interpretation}
\label{note:statistics}

Central comparisons report paired effect sizes, the number of positive cell
or seed pairs, and 95\% intervals unless a different level is specified for
that analysis. Tests were two sided unless a direction had been specified
before analysis. Exact Wilcoxon signed-rank tests were used for
small paired cell cohorts. Reconstructed-cell intervals resampled cells first
and, where relevant, one Monte Carlo stream within cell. Measured-response
intervals resampled target cells. Artificial-network summaries used
independently initialized training runs. Inhibitory-census outcomes were first
averaged within postsynaptic target. Signed-credit intervals and exact sign
tests used animals; neuron-level source rows were descriptive only.

Bootstrap intervals used 20,000 draws unless an experiment's protocol states
otherwise; the Fashion-MNIST ladder, the physical-depth, H4 and task-family
cohorts and the external animal reanalysis used 50,000 paired draws.

Benjamini--Hochberg correction was applied within each prespecified comparison
family---the archived four-budget subtree scan across $K=1,2,4,8$, the six
bandwidth-matched routing contrasts and the H4 primary family. The additional four-budget ancestry-minus-best-control reanalysis enumerated
all $2^{20}$ paired sign assignments for a two-sided mean-difference test
and applied Holm correction across the four budgets. Accuracy differences
were reconstructed on the exact $1/2048$ test-count grid before testing.
Initialization-score correlation intervals used 5,000 whole-seed bootstrap
draws. The local-context soft-encoder analysis used Holm correction across its
three primary contrasts; the hard-readout follow-up remains exploratory. The prospective selection experiment resampled 20 whole seed blocks 10,000
times. The finite-horizon, morphology-calibration, credit and conductance studies
also used 10,000 whole-seed draws, retaining task, candidate and optimizer
conditions together within each seed. The two primary comparisons in each of the finite-calibration and
separate end-to-end experiments used Bonferroni-adjusted 97.5\% intervals and
a prespecified 0.01 improvement margin in normalized MSE (NMSE). Other bridge
intervals are pointwise and descriptive. Exhaustively enumerated structural targets have
no biological sampling interpretation. Response-baseline sensitivity retained seven
targets and 20,000 draws. Other parameter
sweeps and sensitivity analyses received no multiplicity correction, are
labeled secondary, and are not interpreted as independent confirmation.

For the cross-animal structural check, the mouse is the replication unit.
Contrasts were first summarized within each volume; no cell-level test was
used to claim an animal-level $P$ value from two mice. The 12-cell Pinky
cohort was sized to span 12 equal-count spatial strata while keeping complete
mesh skeletonization and 500-shuffle processing feasible. The task-family
factorial used ten paired seeds, matching the sample size used for the
physical-depth experiments; positive directional language
requires a paired 95\% interval excluding zero and at least 8/10 seed signs.

Cross-architecture conclusions exclude pooled error-field metrics that mix
dendritic stages with a somatic stage whose reconstruction is exact by
construction. They also exclude unweighted path-gain dispersion as evidence
of gradient direction and post hoc comparisons between unmatched training
cohorts. Table~\ref{tab:claim_boundaries} records the supported
interpretation and boundary of each evidence class.

Every empirical main panel is accompanied by a machine-readable source table containing
the plotted values, biological or training unit, nesting variables, seed,
condition names, and analysis version. Automated reproducibility checks verify
that figure scripts read these tables rather than duplicating values in plotting code.
Authentication tokens, restricted caches, and raw data already hosted by
MICrONS or DANDI are not redistributed.

The release also includes the three regular-tree sweep files,
representative resolved configurations, the versioned run manifest,
the seed-level result tables, and an executable NumPy reference for the raw
additive forward model and derivatives. Exact source copies of the morphology,
routing-capacity, focal-perturbation and projection-bound analyses are provided
with SHA-256 provenance. The measured-response directory contains the CAVE
join, extraction of Neurodata Without Borders (NWB) files from DANDI, and
task-derived learning scripts together with a
machine-readable four-channel specification and target manifest. The expanded
release also contains the stable-ID inhibitory-census join,
coordinate-transform checks, target-level topology tables, the verified
Francioni source checksum and extracted contrasts, and the
static interference verification grid. Private
filesystem paths in portable configurations were replaced by paths relative
to the article directory; numerical settings were not changed. The provenance
manifest records each origin and every portability edit.

An exact package lock was not archived for the June exact-transport or
backpropagation jobs, nor for the measured-response analysis. These versions
are therefore not guessed from the current environment. The complete July feedback analysis records Python 3.10.13, its Git identity
and selected source hashes. Its archived source state nevertheless includes
tracked modifications. Table~\ref{tab:regular_archive}
and the accompanying reproducibility README distinguish exact, partial and
unverifiable provenance fields.

1.3066--1.3642. At unit dose, the shunt-minus-current localization contrast
was 0.3817 with a cell-bootstrap interval of 0.356--0.409. At every dose and in every cell, all modeled descendant
gradients were attenuated without enhancement or sign reversal. Draws are
nested sensitivity samples, not independent biological replicates.
\end{table}

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering
\caption{Architecture and data specifications for the regular-tree MNIST feedback experiments. The branching vector is ordered proximal to distal. E/I denotes excitatory/inhibitory; TopK selects the active contacts from separate candidate arrays for each sign. Active contacts and total trainable candidate parameters are reported separately.}
\label{tab:regular_architecture}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{p{0.24\textwidth}X}
\toprule
Component & Specification \\
\midrule
Data & MNIST tensors; 784 flattened pixels; no normalization or augmentation; seed-controlled 48,000/12,000 train/validation split; official 10,000-image test set. \\
Modeled population & One layer of 128 excitatory modeled neurons; no separate inhibitory-neuron population. \\
Tree & $[3,3]$: nine distal, three proximal and one somatic compartment per neuron; no somatic input synapses. \\
Input contacts & Per dendritic compartment, separate TopK arrays selected 40 excitatory and 20 inhibitory inputs from 784 candidates per sign; 92,160 active contacts among 2,408,448 candidate synaptic parameters. \\
Parameterization & Nonnegative synaptic and child-coupling conductances obtained by softplus transformation; inactive local weights were not updated. \\
Reactivation & Learned $[\tanh(m(V-b))+1]/2$ at distal, proximal and somatic stages. Shunting used analytical initialization; raw-additive used 0.1/0.9 occupancy-quantile calibration. \\
Readout & Linear map from 128 soma activities to 10 class scores. \\
Model size & 2,414,602 trainable raw parameters in the feedback experiments, of which 2,408,448 were candidate synaptic parameters. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering
\caption{Training protocols for the regular-tree results. Each protocol used minibatches of 256, categorical negative log likelihood, no weight decay, no mixed precision and restoration of the best validation checkpoint. 3F and 5F denote three- and five-factor local updates; ReLU denotes rectified linear unit. TopK rates apply to the candidate synaptic parameters, whereas child-coupling, reactivation and decoder rates apply to their separately listed parameter groups.}
\label{tab:regular_training}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{p{0.15\textwidth}p{0.10\textwidth}p{0.16\textwidth}p{0.19\textwidth}X}
\toprule
Protocol & Seeds; epochs & Core output & Rule and feedback & Adam learning rates \\
\midrule
Original feedback ladder (S45) & 42--56; 180 & Identity & 3F, local decoder; strict scalar feedback, neuron-specific sharing or exact path transport & TopK $1.5\times10^{-3}$; child coupling $7\times10^{-4}$; reactivation $5\times10^{-4}$; decoder $1.5\times10^{-3}$ \\
Matched-width scalar-fallback comparison & 42--56; 180 & Identity & 3F, local decoder; matched-width scalar-fallback feedback & Same as the original feedback ladder \\
Exact path transport factorial & 42--46; 200 & ReLU & 3F or 5F crossed with local or backpropagated decoder; exact path-transported compartment error & TopK $1.5\times10^{-3}$; child coupling $7\times10^{-4}$; reactivation $5\times10^{-4}$; decoder $1.5\times10^{-3}$ \\
Backpropagation reference & 42--46; 200 & ReLU & Standard backpropagation through the matched shunting tree and decoder & TopK and decoder $10^{-3}$; child coupling and reactivation $5\times10^{-4}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}

\vspace{3pt}
\footnotesize Adam used $(\beta_1,\beta_2)=(0.9,0.999)$ and
$\epsilon=10^{-8}$. The 3F local-gradient elementwise clip was 5. The
exact path transport and backpropagation protocols shared architecture, seeds,
epoch budget, and checkpoint-selection procedure, but used the method-specific
learning rates listed above. Both were separate from the 180-epoch
feedback-definition protocol.
\end{table}

\clearpage
\begingroup
\small
\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.22\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.70\textwidth}}
\caption{Notation used in the theory and analyses. Symbols with more than one context-specific role are distinguished in their definitions; feedback-channel count, algebraic rank, routed depth and physical depth are separate quantities.}\label{tab:notation}\\
\toprule Symbol & Definition \\ \midrule
\endfirsthead
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textit{Supplementary Table~\thetable\ continued}}\\
\toprule Symbol & Definition \\ \midrule
\endhead

$u$, $n$, $i$ & Indices for a neuron, compartment and synapse, respectively. \\
$V_n$, $a_n$ & Conductance-stage voltage and transmitted activity of compartment $n$; $a_n=f_n(V_n)$. \\
$g_{c\to n}^{\mathrm{den}}$ & Nonnegative child-to-parent coupling conductance. \\
$\mathcal S_n^{\mathrm E}$, $\mathcal S_n^{\mathrm I}$ & Excitatory and inhibitory contact sets on compartment \(n\); contact class is fixed. \\
$g_i$, $x_i$, $g_ix_i$ & Nonnegative maximal synaptic conductance, presynaptic activity and resulting open conductance. These are distinct from bold gradient symbols. \\
$E_i^{\mathrm{rev}}$, $E_{\mathrm E}$, $E_{\mathrm I}$, $E_{\mathrm L}$ & Contact-specific, excitatory, inhibitory and leak reversal potentials. E/I polarity is set by reversal, not by a signed conductance. \\
$G_n^{\mathrm E}$, $G_n^{\mathrm I}$ & Aggregate activity-weighted excitatory and inhibitory open conductances. \\
$g_n^{\mathrm{tot}}$, $R_n^{\mathrm{tot}}$ & Total compartment conductance and its reciprocal input resistance. \\
$\delta_u$ & Exact neuron-level learning signal for point neuron $u$. \\
$N_u$ & Number of routed nonsomatic compartments in neuron $u$. \\
$A_u$, $\bm c_u$ & Fixed $N_u\times K$ compartmental address matrix and example-dependent vector of $K$ signed channel values; $\widehat{\bm\delta}^{V}_u=A_u\bm c_u$. Fresh MNIST projections instead deliver activation errors, $\widehat{\bm\delta}^{a}_u=A_u\bm c_u$, retaining activation derivatives in eligibility. \\
$\delta^V_{0,u}$, $\delta^V_{n,u}$ & Exact somatic and compartment voltage-space errors for neuron $u$. \\
$\alpha_n^{\mathrm{cond}}$, $\widetilde\alpha_n$ & Conductance-only and effective path gains; the latter includes reactivation derivatives. \\
$\overline\alpha_w$, $\mathrm{CV}_w$ & Eligibility-energy-weighted mean and coefficient of variation of the effective path gain. \\
$\alpha$, $\rho$, $a$ & Sensor alignment in the physical-depth task, second-moment mixture coefficient in the spectral experiment, and imposed route-span energy fraction. \\
$\chi$, $\chi_c$ & Credit-conflict probability in the branch-conflict task and its centered mean-field boundary. The archived run configurations denote the same dose by $\alpha$. \\
$\bm w$, $\bm g$, $\widehat{\bm g}$ & Parameter vector, exact population gradient, and its unbiased stochastic estimate. \\
$\bm q$ & In reciprocal cables, loss sensitivity to injected current. In the algebraic and directed-conductance comparisons, $q_n=\partial u_0/\partial u_n$ or $q_n=\partial v_s/\partial v_n$ is a root-output path derivative; multiplying by scalar output error gives credit. \\
$\bm d$, $\bm m$, $V$, $U$ & Routed-update estimator, its conditional mean, its centered covariance, and optimized nonnegative-step bound in Eq.~\ref{eq:s_operator_utility}. \\
$M$, $\Sigma$, $U(M)$ & Fixed parameter-gradient operator, exact-estimator noise covariance, and the moment score in the special case $\bm d=M(\bm g+\bm\xi)$. \\
$K$, $\operatorname{rank}(\Phi)$ & Number of fixed feedback profiles and their effective algebraic rank; redundant columns need not increase rank. \\
$d_{\epsilon,u}$, $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ & Smallest tested dictionary budget attaining aggregate capture $1-\epsilon$, and participation rank $(\sum_j\lambda_j)^2/\sum_j\lambda_j^2$ of the stated second moment. \\
$H_{\mathrm c}$, $D_{\mathrm{r}}$ & Task-credit hierarchy and routed address depth in the fixed-state signal--noise experiment. \\
$H_{\mathrm p}$, $D_{\mathrm{p}}$; D1--D4 & Physical task hierarchy and physical dendritic stage count. D1--D4 mean $D_{\mathrm{p}}=1,\ldots,4$ only within the stated cohort; the associated branching vectors can differ between cohorts. In $[b_1,\ldots,b_{D_{\mathrm{p}}}]$, factors are ordered soma-to-distal. \\
$A_T$ & Binary synapse-by-route ancestry matrix of tree $T$. \\
$\Gamma_{ik}$, $\Gamma_u$ & Rectangular anatomy-derived route profile from route $k$ to site $i$, and diagonal channel-gain matrix in the main delivery model, respectively. A passive focal shunt has a diagonal gain form in its baseline-weighted ancestry partition, not in every selected route dictionary. \\
$\Phi_{T,K}$ & $K$-channel feedback dictionary implemented by topology, route gains, channel allocation and weights. \\
$D_e$, $U$ & Diagonal eligibility/anatomical-weight matrix in the route dictionary and orthonormal tree-Haar basis, respectively. \\
$W$ & Positive diagonal coordinate-weight matrix; summed excitatory synaptic area in the structural MICrONS analysis. \\
$P_{\Phi,W}$, $\overline P_\Phi$ & Weighted projector onto $\operatorname{col}(\Phi)$ and its ordinary Euclidean representation in coordinates transformed by $W^{1/2}$. \\
$\mathcal C_K$ & Per-field capture or aggregate energy capture at budget $K$, as specified; neither is the directional $\cos^2$ diagnostic. \\
$\bm t_u$, $\overline\Sigma_t$ & Exact target credit field over the compartments of neuron $u$ and its uncentered second moment in the $W^{1/2}$-transformed coordinates. \\
$G$, $\mathcal R$, $R_a$, $R_m$ & Passive conductance matrix, inverse response operator $\mathcal R=G^{-1}$, axial resistivity, and specific membrane resistance. \\
$\kappa_k$, $\kappa_b$ & Focal shunt conductance at compartment $k$ and branch-specific reliability shunt, respectively. \\
$B$ & Number of branches in the branch-conflict task; mini-batch size only when used as the subscript in $\langle\cdot\rangle_B$. \\
$S_K$, $\overline\Sigma_g$ & Feedback-channel assignment matrix in Eq.~\ref{eq:s_dictionary} and transformed uncentered gradient second moment, respectively. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\begin{table}[p]
\centering
\caption{Dictionary constructions, coefficient sources and forward models. These constructions represent a delivered field as a linear combination of profiles but differ in coordinates, admissible profiles, metric and delivered information. A dictionary may be redundant; its rank need not equal its column count.}
\label{tab:feedback_objects}
\footnotesize
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.17\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.29\textwidth}X}
\toprule
Object & Coordinates, profiles and rank & Coefficients, metric, state dependence and forward model \\
\midrule
Compartment addresses \(A_u\)
& \(N_u\) nonsomatic compartments by \(K\) delivery profiles; rank at most \(K\). Shared and subtree indicators are nonnegative; the general linear model allows signed profiles.
& Image interventions route output-activation errors, retaining output derivatives in eligibility; voltage-space capture is separate. Signed example-dependent coefficients and Euclidean field capture. A layer scalar shares a coefficient across neurons; neuron-specific feedback retains ownership. Fixed profiles differ from exact state-dependent path transport of one somatic error. Directed conductance and raw-additive forward trees. \\
\addlinespace
Haar basis / projector \(P_K\)
& Sixteen abstract gradient coordinates; selected orthonormal columns have rank \(K\). The global mode is nonnegative and contrast modes are signed.
& Exact or noisy projected gradient coefficients in Euclidean coordinates; optional fixed reliability gains. Profiles stay fixed within a condition. Quadratic objective with no simulated forward neuron. \\
\addlinespace
Context field \(\Phi^{(K)}\)
& Eight contexts by eight terminal blocks, rank \(K\), unit row norm. Ancestry fields are nonnegative; dense controls may be signed. Nonzero support and assignment vary by control.
& Each trial delivers \(\delta_i\Phi^{(K)}_{c_i,:}\); local input eligibility produces the parameter update. Euclidean directional and update-moment diagnostics differ from span capture. Context-gated logistic block model; internal feedback-tree nodes have no forward state. \\
\addlinespace
Multi-affine path profiles
& Six nonsomatic output coordinates for the matched pairwise/quartic comparison; unit or signed initial profiles each have rank one.
& Fixed-profile learning multiplies one somatic error by the supplied profile. The best rank-one direction and per-example amplitudes are fitted separately for diagnostics. Local eligibilities and forward coefficients can change during learning. \\
\addlinespace
Conductance three-pattern control
& Six voltage coordinates and three disjoint, initial-profile-weighted supports: the two terminal groups and the proximal pair.
& Per-example least-squares coefficients use the current exact field; local synaptic eligibilities remain exact. The binary-context task has at most two path profiles at fixed parameters. Directed E/I balance with all 24 conductances trainable. \\
\addlinespace
Anatomical dictionary \(A_K\)
& Input-bearing segments by \(K\) nonnegative profiles. Historical analyses selected \(K\) descendant supports; the common-mode comparison uses one constant plus \(K-1\) supports. Realized rank can be below \(K\).
& Free fitted coefficients measure representational capacity under mapped excitatory-area weighting \(W\). No training or biological coefficient estimator. Targets are independently modeled reciprocal-cable response fields or explicitly route-generated positive controls. \\
\addlinespace
Functional dictionary \(D\)
& Mapped partner-input coordinates by four selected weighted routes, with effective rank matched to controls.
& Historical training projects baseline transfer once, \(v_D=P_Dv_0\), then delivers \(\delta_i^{\rm out}v_D\): one trial-varying scalar and one fixed profile. New trialwise field and eligibility-weighted update oracles assess capacity only. Complete reciprocal passive tree with measured visual-response inputs. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

\begingroup
\footnotesize
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.05}
\begin{longtable}{p{0.18\textwidth}p{0.35\textwidth}p{0.39\textwidth}}
\caption{Supported conclusions and unresolved claims for each evidence class. Scope is limited to the stated models, tasks, information sources and sampling units; a positive result in one class does not establish the stronger claim in the final column.}
\label{tab:claim_boundaries}\\
\toprule
Evidence & Supported conclusion & Not established \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\multicolumn{3}{l}{\textit{Table~\thetable\ continued}}\\
\toprule
Evidence & Supported conclusion & Not established \\
\midrule
\endhead
Exact factorization & Conductance-tree gradients separate local eligibility from transported compartment error. & A biological circuit computes exact gradients or has exact path transport. \\
Regular-tree feedback & Preserving neuron-specific information closes most of the scalar-feedback gap in both tested architectures; exact path transport makes 3F sufficient; feedback quality orders norm-matched one-step progress. & Universal advantage of shunting, or superior accuracy, memory or latency relative to backpropagation. \\
Branch-conflict path demand & Branch-selective information becomes useful when off-route eligibilities conflict; the shared-credit failure boundary moves with branch count as predicted. & A dendrite-exclusive mechanism, a need for multiple feedback errors rather than an equivalent eligibility gate, or prevalence of this regime in natural tasks. \\
Subtree-address factorial & Task-aligned nested ancestry has a small advantage over matched non-anatomical routes at intermediate bandwidth and over a rewired tree at intermediate budgets. & A uniquely dendritic advantage; matched gated point and flat implementations reproduce the same result, and unrestricted low-rank routes win at the smallest budgets. \\
Moment-bound theory & Update moments bound one-step loss change. The original scalar endpoint selector loses to rank-only and fixed baselines; a separately frozen finite-horizon forecast improves on fresh seeds but competes with a cheap observed-count baseline. & General-purpose fine-morphology selection, universal superiority to exact gradients or a finite-training optimality guarantee. \\
Multi-affine structure & Identical target input-sensitivity spectra can coexist with different tree compatibility; cut matrices with their constant inside row removed exactly characterize representation in the affine-in-child scalar model. & A theorem for arbitrary nonlinear or conductance trees, or equality of model parameter-gradient moments. \\
Finite noisy morphology selection & A generic sparse interaction estimator improves two prespecified pooled comparisons within the declared eight-variable task mixture; a separate cohort confirms estimated-tree gradient learning. & Arbitrary-function or high-dimensional guarantees, an isospectral claim for the random-coefficient mixture, or uniform family superiority. \\
Actual credit learning & Frozen Adam recipes interact with task family on estimated trees; additional oracle-compatible controls retain all rules and rates. & An identical-rate or optimizer-independent credit intervention, universal superiority of subtree profiles, or a biological coefficient estimator. \\
Matched credit geometry & Pairwise and quartic targets share input spectra and a compatible tree but develop different credit spectra. Exact credit substantially improves quartic learning over tested fixed profiles in fresh paired fits, including a common-rate comparison. & A task-only initialization spectrum, proof of minimum external feedback rank, or optimizer-independent asymptotic necessity. \\
Conductance grouping & Fixed physical shape with mismatched input access creates omitted interactions and a population obstruction; exact-gradient grouping effects persist on fresh seeds. & A universal morphology theorem, learned physical-depth optimality, or necessary precise credit under Adam. \\
Exact-target construction & Complete Fourier access gives bounded-coefficient exact trees and certifies minimum depth within the stated class. & A selector inferred from limited calibration data, a universal task-complexity law, or biological discovery of optimal morphology. \\
Physical-depth controls & Serial divisive composition helps when task factors match physical order. H4 saturates at D3 under BP; flexible point MLPs beat serial trees. Extending training preserves the depth benefit but reverses the LocalCA accuracy advantage of exact credit, which retains lower cross-entropy. & A dendrite-exclusive expressivity advantage, natural-task prevalence, biological availability of exact path transport or optimizer-independent equivalence of BP and LocalCA. \\
State-matched reliability conductance & Fixed positive shunts approximate the step-consistent oracle reliability ordering and outperform global, shuffled and anti-aligned shunts under heterogeneous branch noise. & Exact block-scalar attenuation, autonomous shunt learning, a dendrite-exclusive operation or sustained final-loss superiority over unshunted noisy learning. \\
Same-span coefficient learning & Coordinate conditioning creates the finite-data bias--variance crossover predicted by exact linear risk, despite identical route capacity. & A biological Gram preconditioner, unconditional benefit of orthogonal or ill-conditioned coordinates, or new address span. \\
Conductance-valid artificial-tree analyses & Neuron-specific, assignment, spatial and fixed-budget conclusions persist in the 1,000 included artificial-tree runs; the inhibitory-dose family is excluded. & Any claim derived from signed synthetic inputs driving positive-conductance shunting cells. \\
Fixed spatial topology & Spatial partitioning improves forward learning and input coverage at fixed contact count. & Task-aligned dendritic credit routing; the effect persists under backpropagation and a randomly projected task. \\
MICrONS capacity & With a budgeted common broadcast, ancestry captures additional independent modeled response variation; the disjoint 47-cell cohort favors ancestry over depth bins and degree--depth surrogates. Cohort-specific rank and cost differences are retained. & Endogenous task-gradient alignment or learning in the recorded mice; a universal fine-topology advantage independent of field, rank and wiring cost. \\
Inhibitory census & Inhibitory classes occupy different dendritic scales and repeated contacts are spatially co-targeted; actual-route dictionaries are internally compressible. & Geometry-independent descendant-route organization, inhibitory activity, a teaching signal, or biological selection of leverage-ranked routes; all targets come from one mouse. \\
Focal perturbation & In the normalized proxy, an adjoint-replacement control attributes descendant localization to changed adjoint transport; physical calibration specifies where the effect is large or small. & That the effect is universal across electrotonic regimes or that inhibitory synapses conveyed teaching signals during the animal's experience. \\
Weak-channel focal check & Descendant-enriched attenuation persists in the fixed local Jacobian of 512 accepted weak-channel steady states near the passive baseline. & Strongly regenerative operation, channel kinetics, spikes, cell-specific channel fits or independent biological replication by the nested draws. \\
Measured responses & Ancestry--response similarity shows no preferential alignment in seven targets across thirteen scans. The separate 520-fit learning comparison diagnoses restriction of a nearly fixed transfer profile to incompletely covering routes. & Absence of topology--function organization in cortex, failure of every state-dependent coefficient rule, or generalization to other response windows, coverage and biophysical calibration. \\
Projection coefficients & Oracle projections measure capacity; a separately calibrated local-context encoder improves learning over a frozen profile when an explicit cue and route-activation targets are supplied. & Autonomous discovery of the cue or target coordinates, or a demonstrated biological circuit implementing oracle coefficients. \\
Alignment-controlled optimization & Rotating exact credit into a fixed reconstructed-tree basis changes both capture and loss reduction under matched quadratic objectives. & That the reconstructed cells acquired or used the imposed gradients in vivo. \\
Signed animal contrast & Known P+/P- causal sign predicts the dominant dendritic population contrast across six animals. & Within-tree routing or a unique conductance/shunting mechanism. \\
Interference identity & Route overlap and off-route leakage are sufficient to produce exact one-step forgetting in a static quadratic task. & Temporal neural dynamics or a causal explanation of the published motor-learning experiment. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

0.4 & Ancestry shuffle & 0.172 & 0.359 \\
\midrule
1.0 & Ancestry-selected paths & 1.000 & 1.000 \\
1.0 & Random paths & 0.290 & 0.460 \\
1.0 & Depth bins & 0.247 & 0.444 \\

\end{tabular}

\vspace{4pt}
\footnotesize At 40\% alignment, morphology capture exceeded the three
controls by 0.203--0.228 and the difference was positive in 8/8 cell means.
At zero alignment morphology lost to all controls in 8/8 cells; at complete
alignment it exceeded all controls in 8/8 cells. Monte Carlo streams and
gradient fields are nested sensitivity samples, not biological replicates.
\end{table}

Selected and common-rate views reuse 50 coincident fits; twelve short implementation canaries are excluded. Initial/final exact checkpoints supply a separate capture diagnostic. \\
Random between-neuron feedback & 15 seeds per dynamics; 120 new fits, with frozen exact-readout references & SI Fig. S30; feedback matrix fixed within run. \\
Depth, ownership and input topology & 480/640 depth fits; 120/160 ownership; 240/320 spatial; 160/320 fixed-budget fits retained & SI S5; Table S20 records conductance-valid exclusions. Separate 320-fit exact/BP rerun. \\
Branch-conflict selection & 20 seeds, three branch counts, eight nested conflict doses & SI S5 and Fig. S29; paired inputs and masks; six-checkpoint gradient audit on original seeds. \\
Two-branch / eight-context routing & 10 seeds for two branches; 20 seeds and 2,700 fits for full factorial & SI S5; Tables S1--S2. Equivalent implementations are controls, not replication. \\
Local context-coefficient encoder & 20 fresh seeds; 2,160 primary fits; paired exploratory hard-readout sensitivity & SI S5, Fig. S32; separate calibration cues; encoder frozen before task learning. \\
Moment, reliability and coefficient screens & 50 seeds per screen; physical fixed reliability: 2,250 outcomes; adaptive: 1,050; same-span: 4,800 checkpoint rows & SI S4; Tables S3--S6. Seed is the unit; checkpoints remain nested. \\
Prospective linear selection & Original: 5 development / 20 held-out seeds, 12,800 confirmatory fits; new dynamics: 20 fresh seeds, 25,600 fits & SI S4, Figs. S35, S38--S39; independent calibration and sealed choices. \\

Another 640 trajectories test parameter bounds on the same opposed-tuning blocks; 288 additional development fits test an expanded rate grid (Fig.~S52). \\

Interaction structure & Census of 140 primary and 24 nested targets; 7,872 fits and 164 exact constructions & SI S4, Figs. S36--S37; full-domain capacity analysis, not sampled generalization. \\
Structure and learning studies & Twenty paired seed blocks each: 2,240 calibration, 3,600 credit and 1,440 conductance fits; 480 further end-to-end fits & SI S4, Figs. S40--S42; frozen choices, full controls and independent test outcomes. \\
Boolean structure and learning & Seven fixed truth tables; all 15 trees; 20 new seed blocks and 6,720 fits & SI S4, Figs. S43--S44; exact capacity, frozen optimizer recipes and separate classification metrics. \\
Physical depth and point controls & Ten seeds per cohort: H3 270 fits; point/optimizer controls 200; alignment dose 90; grouped H3/H2 220; H4 360; task-family 360 & SI S5, Figs. S15, S28, S31. Anatomical dictionaries & Eight initial and 47 disjoint cells, one mouse; 12 selected/10 eligible Pinky cells, a second mouse & SI S6--S7; Tables S24--S28. Cohorts analyzed separately. \\
Anatomical common broadcast & 65 modeled operators, 149,331 dictionary evaluations & SI S7; frozen follow-up on disjoint 47 cells and eligible Pinky cells; 200 randomized controls per cell. \\
Inhibitory census & 20 postsynaptic targets; 92-axon sensitivity aggregation, one mouse & SI S8; Tables S31--S32. Synapses and connections nested. \\
Passive / weak-channel perturbations & Eight initial cells; 45/47 disjoint cells eligible; weak channels: 64 draws per initial cell & SI S9; Tables S7, S27--S28. Sites and channel draws averaged within cell. \\
Measured responses & Seven targets, 13 scans, ten splits; complete tree: 520 fits; oracle geometry: 130 exact reexecutions & SI S10; Tables S8--S9, S29--S30. Reexecution discrepancies retained. \\
Measured-response baselines & Same 130 splits, 13 scans, seven targets; 3,380 new evaluations & SI S10, Fig. S34; nested ridge tuning and training-only masks; draws averaged before targets. \\
